+## 13. SEC4: independent intent and usable default campaigns (2026-10-19)
+
+Implementer: Codex, SEC4, 2026-10-19. Wave 18 found 192/231 row
+invariants copied enforcement, no audit use, and one campaign run. DX4 owns
+staging discovery/provisioning and QRY owns query return/hidden lookup semantics.
+
+### 13.1 Intent and diagnostics
+
+An advisory N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE identifies table/column effect intent
+whose bound predicate and effective gate imply each other in the existing
+three-valued kernel. Equality is advice only: neither the note nor a generated
+candidate establishes independent specification intent. Keep existing valid
+assertions and proof statuses; do not reject a legitimate simple owner policy.
+Suggest reviewing the intended role, resource relationship, sensitive column
+and lifecycle condition. Draft candidates are explicitly unconfirmed questions
+with per-role/per-column policy alternatives, never copies of the effective
+gate. Where no independent candidate is available, emit a question rather than
+inventing intent. Starters explain the product requirement and show a distinct
+policy decomposition (for example member/admin alternatives).
+
+### 13.2 Generated initial worlds and password-only test
+
+Without explicit fixture files, generate isolated deterministic typed fixtures:
+two distinct principals per discovered role and service seat, related resource,
+membership and ownership rows, independent principal fields, enum alternatives,
+and verified image/file uploads. Ordinary seed validation, constraints, defaults,
+maintenance and reference ordering remain mandatory. Declined populations name
+their coverage gap; continue with the validated empty world if no population
+can be admitted. Existing explicit fixtures retain their meaning.
+
+GeneratedFixtureTrace is distinct from AuthoredFixtureTrace: it establishes an
+actual authored operation/read from a validated generated initial fixture,
+**not** an application operation sequence that creates that initial fixture.
+A replayed policy violation from either fixture origin fails acceptance. Seed-
+only and synthetic table probes remain supplemental/non-failing. Every trace
+retains the full initial rows and actual arguments; reports label this origin.
+The same test-only in-memory engine path works with password-only schemas;
+never open development doors or change deployed identity configuration. HTTP
+authentication stays separately exercised through the existing isolated serve.
+A fixture-free default test may pass its generated campaigns without pretending
+that application business cases ran. Saved-source and explicit business-case
+requirements retain their existing contract.
+
+### 13.3 Coverage and bounded checking
+
+Column update intent is spelled `invariant table.column update only P`; existing
+`write` still includes insert and update. Multi-write observations may judge a
+unique explicit target write when other stable writes touch different tables;
+ambiguous repeated target writes and unstable policy dependencies stay unproved.
+Histories interleave actors, argument alternatives, relationship changes and
+successor operations. Media disclosure uses compiler-typed physical payload
+witnesses; alternate-world media comparisons need a coupled verified ledger.
+Role-wide branches gated by row values receive advisory N_ROLE_WIDE_READ when
+the table carries private intent or media; suggest a projection/board table.
+Escalation advice must constrain the assigned role by dominating argument guards
+before naming it; an excluded target is not a witness.
+
+`check` performs the sound static analysis and a bounded concrete campaign;
+work exhaustion is explicit unproved coverage and points to `test`. A shared
+short campaign deadline bounds exploration, including observer/world loops.
+Already replayed violations remain failures; timeout never awards proof.
+Memoizing exact implication input pairs is allowed; the cache is bounded,
+process-local, and stores only the existing kernel's result. No assumptions
+about fixture rows enter static proofs. `test` and `audit` retain the full
+campaign bounds. Target cold/warm median check latency is at most five seconds
+on the fifty wave-18 schemas.
+
+Default check output is one short summary block with invariant counts and at
+most five ranked actionable notes with source lines and concrete next steps.
+Full reasons, individual unproved paths and complete note lists require
+`--verbose` (JSON retains complete structured evidence). Refuted reachable
+witnesses remain visible in compact form. Recommend `snapback4 test` when
+exploration remains and `snapback4 audit` when exposures need review. The
+start packet/cheatsheet teach check → test → audit as the ordinary loop.
+
+### 13.4 Qualification
+
+Pin before/after measurements, all fifty fixture-free campaign outcomes, the
+eleven high-lane spec-intent outcomes, and the 200-schema no-false-refusal
+survey. Preserve frozen lane sources. Run changed snippets/starters through
+packed CLI qualification, the full requested crate/client/differential suites,
+Linux qualification, affected Mac tests, wasm/ref/docs/root checks. Reports
+distinguish actual caught, advisory/partial, and uncovered findings. No release
+version bump, push or publication belongs to SEC4.

### 8.1 Exact conservative boundary (review clarification)

A snapshot-only evaluation NEVER sets refuted. A row-state counterexample must
actually execute Engine::seed_at in an in-memory engine and then read back the
committed image before judging it; defaults, unique/final constraints, typed
validation and the exact text minimum must pass. That is a replay of the trusted
seed ingress in the `always` quantification, labeled `trusted-seed ingress`,
not proof that an ordinary application sequence reaches it. Rejected seeding,
nonlive/expired/retained/captured/derived populations or unsupported validation
are unproved, never refuted. No mutation is inferred from a snapshot.

The concrete evaluator uses the typed Value and exact §3 leaf/connective
semantics; typed Id and Text stay distinct. Its partial unsupported cases
produce unknown/unsupported, never false, including under Boolean connectives.
It supports no clock/temporal expressions in this increment: `now` or temporal
comparisons remain unproved. It does not borrow the host language's truthiness.

Operation proof is confined to one atomic server mutation transaction. The
interpreter/SQLite transaction admits no concurrent application interleaving
between requirements and its writes; opaque/external work is excluded. Leading
requirements contribute only row-independent viewer/literal/positive raw-probe
facts; every probe is caller-filtered at runtime so positivity is essential.
No fact binds an old/next target field, argument alias or captured row. Policy
expressions needing those bindings stay unproved in this reduction. Reject a
require dependency if ANY operation write may touch it, including the target
write itself, both upsert arms, field-authority paths, maintains, sequences and
referential actions. Unknown/helper/opaque write sets or unclassified forms
force unproved; the absence of an explicit IR write is not an absence proof.
These are conservative blockers, not proposed rewrite/lint assumptions.


## 9. First delivery checkpoint (2026-10-18)

The bounded implementation and five rounds of implementation review are retained
under `llp/reviews/3000.036.004-sec-code-*`; both families mark round 5 READY.
Input hashes, six replayed witnesses and 150-schema compatibility/exploration
reports live in `snapback4/reports/20261018-sec/`. Original schemas contain no
invariants, so that corpus run is not a privacy-spec certification. Five repaired
authority schemas pass the generated challenges; 015 is a trusted seed-ingress
witness rather than a repaired ordinary application workflow.

Remaining whole-protocol, aggregate/time, actual unauthenticated HTTP/device
history, tracing and fixture-asset obligations remain explicitly unproved or
not covered. The open follow-up is
[the qualification-gap ticket](../issues/20261018-sb4-security-proof-and-adversarial-qualification-gaps.md).
No zero-security-holes capability claim or publication follows from this delivery.

## 10. SEC2 observer and adoption amendment

Implementer: SEC2, 2026-10-18. This amendment extends, rather than weakens,
the snapshot quantification and the proved/refuted/unproved contract above.
It is a proposal until the independent reviews and implementation evidence
are recorded. STAGE owns HTTP provisioning and password dev doors; RULES5
owns live-parent semantics; FRONT owns general diagnostic triage.

### 10.1 Separate observer obligations

Report individual observer obligations as well as the whole verdict. A proved
component never clears another unresolved component. Ordinary point, scan,
planned union/projection, count, sum and existence reads consume authorized
rows; pure computation over those rows cannot reveal an unauthorized payload.
The reduction concerns logical data, not the already excluded examined-count,
meter, physical timing or the separately unproved global wire activity path.
Context changes, native programs and external workers are never pure reads.

For a maintained count or sum with ordinary bare group keys, pull the derived
read predicate back to the source row by substituting its group columns.
Prove that predicate implies the source invariant using the existing exhaustive
kernel. Do not assume the source read rule protects an explicitly broader
aggregate. Lost source fields, value-dependent derived rules, aliases,
row-keyed/private/captured maintenance, temporal contributions and downstream
derived observers without a checked substitution stay unproved with the exact
edge and an author repair. A filter can narrow contributors, but is not used
as an assumption in this first reduction. No maintenance consistency axiom is
added to the propositional kernel.

Generate paired in-memory engine worlds, identical except for one protected
row, only when both worlds seed and validate and the privacy policy is false
in both. Execute the same authored operation with identical arguments/viewer
and clock. Different returned data or refusal code/message is a concrete
logical-existence counterexample; ignore sequence numbers, receipts, meters
and timing. Keep both executions and inputs in the witness. An engine/storage
error or unsupported policy evaluation cannot refute. A passing finite paired
campaign proves nothing. Maintenance is rebuilt independently in each world;
never seed a fabricated derived value. Unsupported referential/final-existence
worlds are declined. This tests count/sum/existence, mutation returns and
collision/refusal observations without guessing which successful call is bad.

### 10.2 Temporal intent

A separate, typed clock reduction accepts Boolean combinations of ordinary
rule predicates and comparisons between server `now` and row time fields.
It adds the actual half-open lifetime predicate (nullable lower/upper bounds
included) to read admission. Clock atoms use a compiler-private identity and
cannot alias user fields. Only equivalent operand orderings canonicalize;
there is no inferred inequality arithmetic. A read opening/expiry component
can therefore be proved independently of sweep execution. Public payload is
hidden before `not before` and after `expire at`, even while physical rows
await cleanup. A job that flips a flag establishes no exact deadline: temporal
intent without a matching lifetime or dominating clock gate stays unproved
or receives an executed between-ticks counterexample. Derived lifetime paths
are not silently covered by the direct-row reduction. Offline time/revocation
retains the existing applied-server-position boundary.

### 10.3 Adoption

Normal check emits one project-level N_SECURITY_INTENT note counting tables
with any effective access rule and no invariant. Generate a separate candidate
draft in `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`, with provenance and
an explicit author-confirmation warning. It is never compiled or accepted
automatically. Candidate read/insert/delete predicates repeat existing rules
as a starting point, not inferred product intent; omit unsupported synthetic
or transition forms with an explanation. Stable content is deterministic and
rewritten only when changed. The note is once per check, not suppressed forever
by hidden state: a changed schema must remain discoverable. An invariant on
one effect does not cover another. Audit ends with conditional role/row/effect
exposures lacking a matching invariant; this is syntactic coverage, never proof
coverage. Unproved and refuted declared effects remain visible in verdicts.

Every added guide/starter form is executed against an installed tarball. The
README and start packet introduce invariants together with rules, within the
existing documentation budget. Candidate declarations require the author to
compare against the app specification, particularly current roles, grants,
counts, time and omitted evidence; copying rules alone cannot catch a wrongly
encoded specification.

### 10.4 Reduction details and review clarifications

The exact live predicate is `(lower IS NULL OR lower <= now) AND
(upper IS NULL OR now < upper)`, using materialized column defaults just as
the runtime does. Tests include equality ticks, nulls and defaults. The clock
symbol cannot be serialized as a runtime RuleTerm: its identity contains a
colon, which Quarry column names cannot contain. Reversal maps `a >= b` to
`b <= a` and `a > b` to `b < a`, without changing strictness. Only the pinned
server lifetime clock enters this theorem; a query's rounded read-time value
and a device's host clock do not establish admission. Raw probes and every
derived lifetime observer remain separate obligations/blockers. Schedules do
not constrain the time between deadline and sweep. Any between-ticks witness
must execute actual engine calls at both clocks from independently validated
seed ingress; snapshot evaluation of `now` alone never refutes.

Both paired worlds use §8.1's actual `Engine::seed_at`, committed read-back,
typed/default/unique/final-existence validation, and exact text minima. The
named read invariant is evaluated for the same viewer and hypothetical
protected row, against each world's committed snapshot and identical clock;
unknown is not false. These are seed-ingress worlds, not a reachability proof.
Engine contexts are authenticated only. HTTP signed-out and offline/device
histories remain separately labeled. Compare typed canonical object values
and engine-defined ordered arrays; do not sort away meaningful order. Decline
generated ids/opaque work, incomplete results and any observation that does
not repeat when recreating each world. Random/store-specific cursors are not
counterexamples. Explicitly record the unproved sequence/receipt/wire boundary
and excluded timing/meter boundary even when payloads agree. Only a change in
the protected row is an experimental input; other seed rows remain identical,
and referentially invalid absence worlds are declined.

Pullback preserves typed Value variants, full probe table/index/key/equality
identity and row images. Only bare identically typed group columns substitute;
derived id/value, reference and old/next fields do not. Kernel node/atom cap
exhaustion is unproved. The direct edge can be proved as a component; any
downstream derivation lacking a transitive substitution blocks the whole
declaration. Do not infer transitive privacy from that component result.

The missing-intent note includes public/allow tables and tables with any
non-deny read/insert/update/delete rule; a wholly denied table needs no note.
It is emitted once in each check result without persistent suppression state,
alongside any distinct broad-read advice. Drafts are ignored generated output,
never source or an inventory leaf; stale content is regenerated, never a check
refusal. Each candidate carries an author-confirmation comment, and the draft
header warns that copied wrong rules cannot catch themselves. Include ordinary
update candidates; explain omitted captured/transition forms. Full table
read/insert/update/delete declarations (and `write` for insert/update) cover
their corresponding effect in the syntactic audit summary; operation/column/
always assertions do not cover a whole table effect. Declared unproved or
refuted effects are still shown in the invariant verdicts.

### 10.5 Generated histories and production HTTP

Paired mutations require a stable privacy policy throughout execution: no
write to the protected table or any policy dependency, no insert/upsert/delete
(including implicit id minting), no implicit maintain/sequence/referential
writes, no assets with uncoupled ledger identities, and no opaque worker/native
context. Query logical payloads use the ordinary deterministic interpreter;
repeatability is an extra rejection check, never a proof of determinism.
Every excluded case is named unproved/not-covered. Owner-field patch authority
counts as effective access in missing-intent discovery and uncovered audit
entries, even if the table update rule denies; include the column and old-owner
predicate. Drafts explain that authority instead of inventing transition syntax.

Default test also explores short actual ordered mutation sequences from a
schema-valid focused fixture, then reads as the named ordinary viewers. The
record includes initial rows, each operation/viewer/typed argument/outcome and
the final ordinary read. Evaluate read intent in the actual post-history state,
never from a role's old grant. General `always` integrity can be refuted on
committed changed row images after an authored operation; no net image is used
to attribute per-write authority. Uncontrolled/implicit write authority still
needs boundary tracing before it can refute operation-specific write intent.
Enumerate declared personas/service seats and Cartesian enum/Boolean argument
tuples, with explicit caps and unvisited coverage; no passing bounded campaign
proves arbitrary histories. Temporal/captured/maintenance/asset-ledger fixture
histories remain not-covered until their complete replay inputs are present.

Signed-out challenges run in a private production `serve` child over the
actual schema, with no dev mode, fixtures or user credentials. Send unauthenticated
and forged-persona HTTP requests to every public query/mutation and verify
E_AUTH/401, plus closed development doors. Native/action/effect applications
requiring app-owned startup artifacts are named not-covered, not represented
by a partial schema host. Preparation/requests are bounded; kill/wait only
recorded children and remove the private temporary directory after process
exit. This qualifies auth admission, not role history or connected-device
revocation; STAGE retains ownership of staging/account provisioning.

The repaired 015 reference workflow records photo-backed placement/transfer/
adoption handoffs in the same ordinary mutation as custody changes; profile
provisioning and all pets/handoffs in its witness use authored operations.
Trusted seed is not used to manufacture the custody counterexample or repair.
General snapshot integrity still quantifies over arbitrary trusted ingress,
so the ordinary workflow test is not an all-ingress custody theorem.

A policy outside the bound rule/clock fragment is never dependency-free: paired
mutations decline it until dependency analysis of the complete retained AST
is available. The count-policy grant-before-read regression checks this
boundary explicitly. General aggregate integrity is interpreted over complete
validated raw snapshots for refutation only (count/sum and exact bounded
integer arithmetic); unsupported filters, time, captures and type coercions
remain unknown. This interpreter never supplies proof assumptions.

## 11. SEC2 reviewed checkpoint

The amendment and implementation received independent **READY** from fresh
Codex and Muse in round 5 at the reviewed source recorded in
`llp/reviews/3000.036.004-sec2.provenance.json`. All five rounds and their
verbatim outputs are retained. Earlier findings tightened dependency stability,
implicit id/asset entropy, bare-key provenance, owner-field discovery and
resource/error observation boundaries. No conservative blocker was converted
into a whole-exposure proof.

The native 20k and both-device 0–999 campaigns retain zero disagreements; the
Lean campaign retains exactly the four existing signature classes/counts.
The 150 original schemas compile with zero diagnostics and still have no
invariants: their generated operation runs are compatibility/exploration.
Installed-doc-only builder and repaired-lane probe evidence, full qualification
results and the remaining limits are indexed in
`snapback4/reports/20261018-sec2/` and the workstream report. This checkpoint
is not a zero-security-holes or publication capability claim.

### 11.1 Avoid duplicate concrete searches

Installed-doc builder measurements exposed minute-long repeated searches of
already-proved direct read/required-column components. Discharge those direct
searches with the existing reviewed component result, without promoting the
whole invariant or suppressing indirect paired/temporal/history/operation
challenges. For paired experiments, compute a fixed-point observer table set
from the protected table through maintained targets, inherited readers and
all table-rule dependencies. Skip authored operations mentioning none of that
set. This prunes finite experiments only; it contributes no proof assumption
and clears no blocker. Unknown/opaque observers retain their existing limits.

Proved ordinary insert/delete components also
need no duplicate direct witness search. Update/both-image claims are not
discharged by a next-image theorem. Before the unchanged bounded enum matrix,
try one row candidate pinned to explicit policy field literals (for example a
cancelled status). This candidate is not a proof fact: typed/default/final
validation, actual engine replay and policy-false judgment are unchanged.
Preserve every previous matrix sample; failed literal seeding only declines
that extra candidate.

For ordinary updates, prove the policy over both old and next images using
`update_rule_judged => policy.old_and_next`. Substitute an OldField with its
next-image Field only for id or an explicitly engine-enforced immutable local
column: every admitted update preserves those values. Never collapse mutable
fields, reference hops or their images. This is the same immutable-image law
used by the engine's once-judged update rule; it is not a new consistency
assumption about database contents. A named transition allowing only the next
owner cannot prove an old-and-next ownership policy. Owned-field bypass and
implicit writers retain their separate blockers and concrete searches. The
reviewed two-image component may discharge duplicate ordinary update/write
searches only where no owned-field bypass exists. Actual authored operation
matrices and unresolved observer obligations still run.

### 11.2 Faithful changed-image witnesses

The installed-doc builder found a misleading synthetic update witness: its
`row` repeated the pre-update row, so replaying that apparent unchanged patch
was refused even though the generator had actually changed status away from
the forbidden old value. Synthetic writes now read back the committed target,
record the actual next image and changed patch, and decline no-op/implicit-only
updates or a missing requested column change. The old image is recorded
separately. Under the existing both-image contract, a false old-image policy
can refute an admitted changed update even when the next image satisfies it;
that is not evidence the unchanged patch was admitted. The installed security
guide now states this distinction explicitly and routes per-operation role
authority to operation-specific declarations. Scope remains schema-valid
seed ingress plus synthetic engine operation, never ordinary app reachability.

Private maintained columns add their owning table, not merely their synthetic
maintain target, to the transitive observer set. Direct-search discharge must
not skip synthesized authored-operation assertions sharing the same source
site. Variant-independent unsupported operation preconditions (opaque body,
row-image policy, ambiguous writes and unstable dependencies) are checked once
before snapshot construction, with the same explicit unsupported reason.
Neither change reduces the previously executable argument/enum matrix.

### 11.3 Actual credential revocation admission

The private production HTTP campaign now authenticates through the schema’s declared guest or password method, confirms live schema admission, rotates that bearer, confirms successor admission, and logs out all sessions. It then requires E_AUTH/401 for both retired credentials at schema, changes, sync, probe, refresh and every authored query/mutation door. Credentials never enter counterexamples or logs. Schemas without ordinary identity and applications needing external startup artifacts retain explicit missing-coverage reasons. This tests connected HTTP admission after credential revocation; it does not assert that a disconnected device erased copied bytes, or establish current-role freshness without a sync round.

The campaign transport decodes HTTP chunk framing before parsing JSON: larger production schema responses use that framing. Truncated, oversized and malformed responses fail qualification rather than becoming a claimed refusal.

Final review hardening: require a distinct same-principal rotation successor and E_AUTH on the old token before logout-all, so mass revocation cannot mask a broken rotation. Declared Content-Length must match the complete received body; duplicate/invalid lengths and conflicting or unsupported transfer framing fail qualification, including a truncated response whose prefix is already complete JSON.

The private transport accepts the host’s declared-length or plain-chunked response fragment only: malformed header names/OWS, bare line breaks, signed/nonhex chunk sizes, unsupported chunk extensions and missing body framing fail qualification. On Darwin, the opened executable fd must match the device/inode of the VM region backing this process’s loaded Mach-O header (PROC_PIDREGIONPATHINFO); copy through that fd and reject a changing snapshot. A replaced pathname can never silently qualify a replacement image. The platform regression replaces a running child’s executable pathname before snapshotting and requires refusal. Unsupported platform/identity inspection fails qualification.

Both session responses must contain explicit nonempty string principal/token fields; missing/null/malformed identities cannot compare equal and qualify a same-principal rotation. Regression covers absent, null, empty, whitespace, non-string and unequal identities as well as an echoed bearer.

### 11.4 Complete compiled seat discovery

The finite cast includes service-seat literals from the compiled schema, authored operation IR and independently declared invariant policies. Table-rule-only discovery missed seats named only in operation require clauses or in intent. Extra service-prefixed data literals conservatively add actors; cast membership never proves principal reachability or creates a proof assumption. Existing casts remain included; larger bounded sequence/enum campaigns retain explicit unvisited reasons. Native/external worker artifacts remain outside this schema-only discovery claim. The matrix regression includes a table-rule-only seat, an operation-only seat and an invariant-only seat with nullable enum combinations.

### 11.5 Reuse pure invalid-world judgments

Within one paired snapshot variant, final-exists validation rejects the same committed fixture independently of the later operation/viewer. Cache only this exact typed structural rejection for that present or absent world, retaining every observer-specific unsupported reason. Never cache an operation result, policy judgment, allocation/storage/resource error or successful observation. Actual admitted calls, counterexample checks and repeatability replays are unchanged. This avoids repeatedly constructing the same invalid world during check; it does not turn missing fixture coverage into a pass. The existing social offline-receipts guard remains unchanged and measures the recovered authoring path.

 succeeded in 0ms:
## 12. SEC3 day-two amendment

Implementer: SEC3, 2026-10-19. Commission: day-two fix list 3/4/5/8.
REL5 owns release transitions and compatibility; MIG owns backfills and staging
clock control. This amendment changes security analysis, not runtime authority.

### 12.1 Column read intent

`invariant table.column read only P` protects the value, computations over it,
value-dependent counts/existence/refusals, copied values, assets and delivery.
It does not forbid observing an otherwise public row's existence independently
of the protected value. Binding validates the column and preserves its identity
in every verdict and audit. Effective payload authority is row read AND any
owner-private field gate. Pure projections excluding the field may eliminate
that payload path; predicates, group/order keys, derived values and copied
fields remain observations. Unknown flow stays unproved. A synthetic whole-row
read cannot refute a projection-only column policy: column counterexamples
execute an authored observer in two schema-valid worlds differing only in that
column, with policy false in both. Outputs must differ reproducibly. Maintained
values are recomputed; column-sensitive counts/existence are included. Separate
obligations name unresolved raw-probe, wire, device and opaque flows. A direct
column theorem never silently clears those obligations. Per-column audit
reports effective audience plus operation/derived observers, not only its type.

### 12.2 Reproducible campaigns and reachability

Generated seed ingress contains only authored base rows/fields. Never re-seed
maintained targets or engine-recorded fields. Generated lifetime samples are
live at the campaign clock; principal fields vary independently. Rejected
snapshots are recorded as unsupported, not successful attempts. Existing
fixture histories instead fork the complete SQLite store and asset ledger into
owned isolated memory stores; captures, deadlines, maintained values, lifetimes
and actual media identities survive unchanged. No source store is modified.
Temporary asset directories belong to Store and disappear on drop.

Actors include all principal values in fixtures. Required/optional references
range over fixture rows, not only the first. Short histories visit first steps
breadth first before extending them under a reported work cap. Read and row
assertions are checked after each step. Write/operation assertions judge only
actual committed changes, correct insert/update/delete arms and pre-write
policy state; ambiguous multiple writes, implicit effects or unstable policy
need executed-effect tracing and remain unsupported. A finite trace is never
an induction proof for always or arbitrary histories.

Every counterexample has a machine-readable origin: authored trace from trusted
fixture, trusted-seed-only, or synthetic operation. Only the first establishes
application reachability from the recorded trusted starting state and fails
normal check/test. Seed/synthetic witnesses remain refutations of all-snapshot
claims, visible with --all and JSON, without failing acceptance. Unproved
reachability is never asserted unreachable as a mathematical theorem. Check
loads the same fixture base for reachable histories as test, without HTTP or
business-case execution. A stronger witness must replace a synthetic witness
for the same declaration. --all exposes all witnesses and never changes which
ones fail. No unreachable refutation may print pass/ok beside exit 1.

### 12.3 Actionable authority advice

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies authored caller operations that write role
or group grants trusted by later rules while the effective admission does not
establish independent authority to confer that particular grant. Include
self-signup roles and transitions where a caller can confer a role they lack.
A trusted service seat or an independently authorized administrator may appoint
other roles; holding an unrelated ordinary role does not establish delegation.
Advice names the operation, grant and gate and points to guide staging's
operator first-admin recipe. It is advice, never an implicit runtime refusal
or a proof of malicious intent. Legitimate open enrollment can be intentional.

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE requires an actually mutable/revocable current-role
population and a retained principal/grant branch lacking that role recheck;
cover read AND write branches. Immutable/static role populations do not fire.
N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE excludes public populations and caller-owned role lookups;
only an absent/hidden distinction crossing a narrower private audience warrants
advice. Every security note has a real source location and contributes to the
reported note count. Rule printing preserves connective precedence. Identity
429 responses include a conservative retry delay covering the rate window;
the existing limits and authentication semantics are unchanged.

### 12.4 Evidence

Qualification uses read-only copies of all ten wave-17 high lanes and their
spec-authored invariant sets, plus all 150 earlier schemas. Report caught,
partial and not caught separately; all-snapshot witnesses alone do not count
as a reachable detection. Preserve bad/fixed controls, note counts and source
hashes. The open qualification-gap ticket remains open for unresolved whole
proof/device/opaque obligations. No zero-hole claim follows from finite tests.


### 12.5 SEC3 reduction details

The direct column theorem is `(row_read AND owner_private_gate) => P` in the
existing three-valued kernel; deny-delete is never an admission for column
read. Whole proof still requires every observer obligation. This increment
uses dynamic column-world observers, not an unimplemented static dataflow
claim: pure projections can avoid a refutation, but do not acquire a proof by
absence of finite counterexamples. Census is existing point/scan/view/filter/
count/sum/exists, mutation return/refusal/copy, asset, sync, raw/derived/opaque
paths. Unsupported field flow remains unproved. Audit's `column_audiences`
maps every visible column name to its effective row-and-private-field rule;
operations and derived edges remain separately enumerated.

Column worlds use the same ordinary viewer, server clock and row id; the named
column has two different schema-valid typed values, and policy is false on its
respective image in each committed world. Every non-native authored query and
deterministic mutation is eligible; fresh ids, effects and incomplete/resource
results are declined. Compare typed logical data and authored refusal messages,
retain ordered arrays, repeat both observations to reject unstable outputs.
Rebuild maintained rows from authored inputs, never accept fabricated derived
or recorded fields. Required media substitutions without a coupled real ledger
remain explicitly unsupported. A primary observation from the exact trusted
fixture is a reachable observation; its synthetic alternate world establishes
value dependency, not reachability of an alternate application history. JSON
retains both worlds and explicitly states this distinction.

`AuthoredFixtureTrace`, `TrustedSeedOnly`, `SyntheticOperation` are the witness
origins (serialized in kebab-case). Status remains refuted for an all-snapshot
counterexample. Normal text labels seed/synthetic refutations non-failing and
hides their large witnesses; --all reveals them and does not change exit status.
Only authored fixture observations fail check/test. A reachable witness wins
over a synthetic witness for the same declaration. With no fixture, check uses
schema-only exploration and names missing application-reachability coverage.
Check discovers an explicit --fixture or project fixtures/; it uses an isolated
in-memory loader at the fixed campaign clock, never the development store.

Candidates sort by operation, argument value and principal. Enumerate bounded
Cartesian arguments including nullable enums and fixture references; interleave
operations for breadth-first first steps, then diagonal ordered pairs. The
512 first-step/512-pair and 1000-fixture-row caps are reported; no exhaustive
claim is made for unvisited combinations. Fixture asset directories are owned
by Store and removed on drop, including error returns. Histories judge direct
single-write net images only; multiple writes, dependency-changing and opaque
boundaries remain unsupported rather than fabricating write-boundary events.

The escalation advisory compares role privileges structurally, never by an
administrator role-name allowlist. Enumerate literals from a role enum or
role probes over a text roster; substitute each caller/assigned role into the
roster write gates and other table-effect gates. A possible ordinary assignment
that adds an effect the caller lacks warrants advice; explicit service-only
branches are excluded from this advisory cast, not from invariant proofs.
Literal assignment targets restrict the target set; dynamic values retain all
candidate roles. This is overapproximate advice: independent operation guards,
intentional enrollment and delegation still need specification review.
Grant-lifetime notes require a role/kind column with an authored update/upsert/
delete and a mutable role field; immutable seed-only populations are excluded.
Existence advice requires a private table lookup, an independent stricter read
policy and distinct later refusal; public and viewer-owned lookups are excluded.
Locations are exact column/rule sites where available, table sites otherwise;
the project-wide missing-intent note uses the first source's line 1.

Identity rate refusals on the existing identity routes use the existing
60-second WINDOW: Retry-After: 60, denied.retryAfterMs: 60000. This is a
conservative wait, not exposure of individual rate-key state. No limit changes.
Qualification inputs are ~/sb4-loop/wave17-repro/sec/<lane>/snapback/security.q
and read-only wave17 app copies; the report pins their hashes. The ten high
lanes are 006/011/013/015/019/021/027/040/045/048. Caught means an authored
fixture observation of the reported bad behavior; partial retains a relevant
note or synthetic witness with a missing affected path. Each of the eight real
highs is scored separately. All eight caught and no false refusals on 150 older
schemas is the target, never inferred from an incomplete run.


### 12.6 Disclosure and clock clarifications

A column observer mutation is declined if it can change the protected image,
policy dependency or an unresolved implicit writer before disclosure. A
pre-operation false policy cannot refute an authorized post-transfer return.
Column observers also run after committed history steps, preserving the
trusted initial fixture and authored prefix. All reference and principal
arguments use the same bounded fixture Cartesian cases; truncation of read
observer cases at 32 is named. Clock-only whole-row probes exclude columns.
Column tick coverage beyond the recorded campaign clock remains named.

All security fixture loading, probes, writes, paired deletions and policy
judgments use the same explicit campaign clock. Business tests keep their own
existing clock and store; security reloads trusted fixture files independently.
Raw policy probes respect both not-before and expire-at bounds. Direct delete
assertions judge removed pre-images against the stable pre-write state, never
surviving rows alone; ambiguous implicit removals remain unsupported.

Fixture forks copy already activated engine state without re-adopting a schema.
They use fresh entropy/minter and private assets, never shared live connections.
Ephemeral-hub fixtures require a separate isolated snapshot and are declined.
Structural privilege advice combines dominating safe operation requirements
with the actual insert/update/upsert gate and applicable owned-field patch gate.
A mutable roster alone is not enough for lifetime advice: require independent
current-role intent or a retained recipient column tied to a role literal
(judge, inspector, host, contractor, engineer; ordinary author attribution alone
is not a current-role contract). Deletion revokes even an immutable role field.

### 12.7 Stable judgments and direct payload witnesses

Unknown bound policy dependencies never authorize a mutation observer: such
column mutations require disclosure-boundary tracing. Pre-write assertions use
a transitive closure of maintained and cascade/set-null writes; a policy
dependency or target that may change implicitly makes the assertion unsupported.
The same rule covers insert, update, upsert and delete. Looped/opaque net images
cannot stand in for executed write events. Effective inserted authority includes
a defaulted role even when omitted from the source write.

An authored complete query returning a compiler-typed Row/Table or Page/Table
containing the protected row id is a direct payload witness at that query's
snapshot. Nested typed row returns retain their source-table identity. This
avoids inventing an invalid absent world by deleting a referenced parent.
Projected scalar/aggregate/refusal observations still require validated paired
worlds; a matching untyped id or value alone cannot attribute an exposure.

Check prioritizes actual fixture histories and column observers before searching
remaining synthetic counterexamples. It does not repeat the test-only operation
exploration matrix, which judges no additional policy. That matrix shares an
8192-call budget across every operation (1–512 calls per operation), reports
truncation, and preserves enum/reference/principal alternatives within that cap.
Required image/file operation arguments are actual uploads owned by the acting
principal, with ledger/scratch custody in the isolated engine. Unsupported video
and uncoupled column-world media identities remain named.

For escalation advice, a role-protected appointer is an explicit delegation
boundary; conferring a different role is not by itself evidence of escalation.
Independent role-value intent may restrict that boundary (for example a producer
must not confer legal). Ordinary role casts make service-seat equality false,
including inside operation requirements. Fixed literals still undergo the privilege comparison; ordinary enrollment
whose rules add no role-specific authority is not advised. Analyze caller-chosen
roles, defaulted inserted authority and owner-field upgrades; respect the
argument's actual enum choices. A fixed bootstrap that can appoint further
roles remains an actionable first-admin risk. Notes remain advisory; absence
of independent intent is not proof that a delegation matches the specification.

### 12.8 Runtime intent and independent worlds

Reference-hop intent is judged by the engine's own authorized RuleProbe and
three-valued verdict on an isolated actual snapshot. Hidden/missing references
remain Unknown, never false; snapshot-only evaluators decline those terms.
Temporal reference policies require a per-tick authorized replay and remain
explicitly unsupported until that path runs. Referenced immutable-column worlds
use fresh stores with copied verified asset ledgers and all authored base rows,
then ordinary seed validation/recomputed maintenance. They do not require a
legal delete transition. Every non-protected base field, capture, row identity
and row population must match before comparing observations.

Implicit stability includes source and member writes to row-keyed views,
maintained owner columns, through-parent effects and transitive cascade/set-null.
Only an actual direct delete can refute delete authority; commit-end expiry is
not relabeled a deletion by the caller. Defaulted upsert roles are analyzed on
the insert arm only; omitted fields are preserved on update. Revocability advice
requires an expanded write to a mutable role field or an admitted deletion,
not an unrelated display-name update.

For a write history, a stable necessary policy component may establish a
violation despite another changing conjunct. Replace unstable atoms (or an
entire unstable negation) by Allow, retaining And/Or composition; separately
verify `original => weakened` with the existing exhaustive kernel. Denial of
that weakened condition establishes denial of the original, never its proof.
Stability includes transitive read-authority dependencies of referenced rows.
Do not use this reduction when implicit writes can change the target image or
executed arm. This catches a demoted recorder whose role stays false even when
another statement updates an unrelated field on its parent. No new axiom is
introduced. Id-column presence/count intent shares the row identity observer
path; arbitrary untyped matching ids do not establish a payload witness.

Independent intent binds authorized reference operands without installing a
runtime read gate; live-hop restrictions on executable read rules do not remove
those operands from intent. Probe-key/equality reference hops participate in
stability and transitive authority dependency collection. Every committed
history state rechecks row assertions, including unchanged rows whose related
policy rows changed; pre-existing unchanged seed violations stay supplemental.
Role advice expands helpers with lexical argument substitution and dominating
requirements, retaining the actual write source site. Constant defaults and
literal assignments use the same authority comparison. Optional-column pairs
vary both values and null presence to observe value-existence aggregates.

Mutation column observers include transitive read-authority dependencies and
implicit targets when establishing policy stability. Role probes normalize
unique-key components and extra equalities identically. Argument-domain
narrowing follows the resolved argument expression, never a coincidentally
same-named argument beside a literal assignment. An unconditional helper call
exports its guaranteed requirement prefix to subsequent caller writes;
conditional/loop-local requirements do not become caller assumptions.

Column observers first judge authorized intent on the validated primary world
once per actor. They share a 16384-call campaign budget (including repeatability
and declined worlds), with explicit remaining-world coverage when exhausted.
The budget cannot discharge an obligation or turn an unproved path into proof.

Only current-role intent establishes that retaining a grant is wrong; naming a
principal column after a role does not. Lifetime advice therefore requires an
independent current-role invariant as well as an authored revocation writer.
The project-wide missing-intent note identifies tables with no invariants; it
does not diagnose a missing current-role clause when other intent is present.
Without independent current-role intent, lifetime advice stays silent: authors
must declare that contract before the checker can distinguish intentional
retained access from a stale grant. Global role
advice requires a principal/role unique roster; per-resource context keys need
a separate delegation reduction. Delegation guards constrain the actual role
column of that roster, never an ordinary project/status lookup. Assignment
intent is selected by effect, with upsert arms judged separately. Initial row
assertion failures remain visible as TrustedSeedOnly, even without mutations.
The operation matrix uses a shared remaining-call counter and fair per-operation
quotas of 1–512; above 8192 eligible operations, unvisited names are explicit.

For fixed ordinary enrollment, row-scoped permissions alone do not warrant
escalation advice. Without contrary independent intent, a fixed assignment
requires new unconditional read/delete or roster authority to emit the note;
caller-chosen roles retain the broader effect comparison. This advisory is not
an all-operation reachability theorem; scoped-resource delegation and fixed
write-only privilege distinctions require independent intent/reductions.
The report keeps the shortest reachable witness. Campaigns can also retain
separate one- and two-step examples without replacing a shorter trace or
attributing a pre-existing disclosure to an unrelated mutation. Named-operation
intent covers all its target-table effects; table/column insert/update/delete
intent covers only that executed arm. Column prefilters and paired intent use
validated committed images; unsupported synthetic operation judgments are named.

History read observers first select compiler-typed row-return candidates or the
transitive maintained/through/raw-probe table closure for scalar observations.
They reserve at most 32768 authored read calls per campaign, including repeats;
remaining disclosures are explicit uncovered work. Mutation sequence replay
continues within its own bounds. Read-call exhaustion never grants a proof.

Tuple-key role tests use the same column-aware normalization in lifetime advice.
A scoped enum context is not a role qualifier; qualifying unique keys contain
one principal and actual role qualifiers only. Named-operation delegation intent
is honored for that caller operation. Initial fixture column observations run
before history prefixes and synthetic exploration; continuation shares the same
work counters so a later phase cannot evict a zero-step witness by consuming its
budget first. §12.8's independent-current-role requirement supersedes the older
name-based alternative in §12.6.

Fixed-role comparison retains any additional probe context as an opaque
predicate; a matching role alone cannot make a region-qualified read globally
unconditional. Lifetime advice excludes a retained branch that already implies
the declared policy, including explicit owner alternatives. Arbitrarily named
role qualifiers require unambiguous literal-probe evidence before a compound
principal/qualifier key can be treated as a global roster.

Synthetic enumeration deduplicates identical candidate seed rows and typed
operation arguments after dependency pruning and literal pinning. Unrelated
schema enum digits cannot cause repeated replay of the same input. This is
finite input exploration, with no exhaustive entropy/generated-id claim.

A different or negated current-role check does not discharge retained-grant
intent: only implication of the applicable independent policy suppresses that
advice. Live roster probes are not themselves described as retained grants.
Repeated risky branches within one table/effect produce one located advisory;
audit retains their individual audiences and writers.

An initial column pass visits every sampled actor within its shared budget,
retaining each actor's first disclosure instead of stopping after the first
viewer. Later unrelated prefixes cannot replace an already observed zero-step
exposure for another sampled viewer.

Unprojected compiler-typed Row/Page payloads also witness column reads when the
returned field equals that committed physical value and its field-visibility
gate admits this viewer. This covers real media ids without manufacturing an
alternate asset ledger. Projected scalar/shape values retain the paired-world
requirement; a coincidentally matching constant is not column provenance. A
private field's redacted null is never treated as disclosure of a null value.

Absent contrary independent intent, caller-chosen role advice requires new
unconditional table authority; an additional trusted resource/ownership grant
is a scoped delegation boundary requiring its own reduction. Fixed enrollment
keeps the narrower read/delete/roster comparison. This avoids treating ordinary
self-enrollment for one's own records as privileged provisioning.

Principal-only global membership grants also participate in advice. An
unqualified viewer probe of a required unique principal denotes current
membership; authored deletion/principal reassignment can revoke it. Membership
creation receives escalation advice when it contradicts a pure caller-based
independent provisioning invariant (table insert/update/write or named operation).
Principal reassignment uses update authority including private-field ownership;
upsert creation and reassignment are judged on their separate arms. A dominating
global appointer role may reside in a separate roster, with all its qualifiers
retained when checking implication.
Ordinary account enrollment without such provisioning intent is not guessed to
be an administrator grant. Scoped/qualified membership still needs its reduction.

Paired query observations tag successful data separately from authored logical
refusals, retaining refusal codes and messages. Only engine-provided authored
rule/constraint markers qualify; incomplete reads, resource exhaustion and
ambiguous engine errors remain outside logical comparison. This prevents both
missed refusal oracles and collisions between a payload shape and a refusal.
Synthetic principal sampling separates the focal row's attribution from the
supporting roster/assignment recipient; finite enumeration still never proves
that all tuples are covered.

Within the unchanged 512-pair cap, up to 256 pairs prioritize a global-role
mutation followed by a distinct operation as the named affected principal.
Ref arguments resolve through fixture roster rows; scalar principal arguments
supply other recipients. First steps and successor operation names interleave;
remaining slots retain diagonal pairs. These are candidates only: real commits,
authorized policies and stable necessary conditions still decide witnesses.

The 16384 column budget counts call/world reservations: each world variant,
authority-judgment actor, visited observer program and generated snapshot reserves
a slot even when it declines before execution; each paired experiment reserves
four calls for primary, alternative and repeats. Typed history payload witnesses
reserve two read calls and require identical complete data on the committed image.
A failed repeat is named unsupported, never a reachable failing counterexample.

Write-arm predecessor matching uses physical unexpired rows, including rows
whose `not before` time is pending; read visibility never converts an update
into insert or hides an executed delete. Every generated and fixture column pair
compares both committed readbacks for non-protected base fields/captures and
populations, and records the actual committed alternate world. Repeatability
also checks those input worlds, not just the returned logical observations.

Whole-row paired observers use typed fixture argument products (32 per observer),
including both synthetic principals independently of the row's owner; state and
argument variants must not accidentally force every aggregate lookup to the
opposite owner. A shared 8192-call reservation budget covers primary/alternate
and repeats; exhaustion explicitly names the remaining uncovered worlds.

Column comparison also requires identical materialized observer arguments across
both worlds and repeats. Independently generated asset-input identities are an
explicit coupled-ledger blocker, not evidence about the protected column.

Time arguments sample one day before the campaign clock, the clock itself and
one day after; this controls input values, not STAGE/MIG's host clock. Singles
also retain successful unique candidates that populate an initially empty
read-protected media table and those that change an existing global role.
Up to 256 pair slots replay media creation then a role change, including changes
by another caller; actual uploaded media and both commits remain in the trace.
Remaining pair slots prefer the role-successor plan above, then diagonal pairs.
The 512-single/512-pair limits do not increase. This exercises stale media grants
without inventing asset-bearing seed rows or silently claiming longer histories.

History read observers materialize actor-owned image/file inputs before querying,
reuse those exact inputs for repeats, and clone the verified ledger into paired
absence worlds. Upload preparation failures and every failed typed/paired repeat
name their operation/invariant coverage blocker; they never silently clear an
obligation. Column pairs still decline independently materialized asset inputs
when their identities differ, as required by their fixed-input comparison.

Authority advice conjoins all supplied owned-field old-owner gates; it does not
OR ownership with the table gate. Only a statically exclusive owned-field patch
may bypass an unnamed update rule. Mixed patches and named transitions retain
the judged table gate, including private fields it needs to inspect. Insert
ownership applies to supplied fields, not omitted defaults. Named next-image
role literals participate in target-role substitution. The same calculation
governs role assignments and principal-only membership reassignment.

An update gate that implies the role/principal is unchanged is not an escalation
witness, even when its source patch explicitly supplies that field. Runtime
regressions cover owned-only, mixed, named-deny, named-no-change and named-target
restrictions for role writes, plus owned principal-only membership reassignment.

A validated empty fixture/store is also a trusted initial state. Histories do
not switch off merely because no authored rows exist: ordinary operations may
create data and subsequent authored queries may disclose it. Such traces retain
their real mutation history and authored-fixture origin. A query-only empty app
still cannot turn arbitrary seeded/synthetic rows into reachable failures. The
packed security-card proof executes its writers from empty state and requires
row and column disclosures to carry that reachable origin when the rule widens.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/effects.rs:263:A held poll is unconfirmed: its push waits five seconds from the commit for a new poll to arrive. ## 12. SEC3 day-two amendment

Implementer: SEC3, 2026-10-19. Commission: day-two fix list 3/4/5/8.
REL5 owns release transitions and compatibility; MIG owns backfills and staging
clock control. This amendment changes security analysis, not runtime authority.

### 12.1 Column read intent

`invariant table.column read only P` protects the value, computations over it,
value-dependent counts/existence/refusals, copied values, assets and delivery.
It does not forbid observing an otherwise public row's existence independently
of the protected value. Binding validates the column and preserves its identity
in every verdict and audit. Effective payload authority is row read AND any
owner-private field gate. Pure projections excluding the field may eliminate
that payload path; predicates, group/order keys, derived values and copied
fields remain observations. Unknown flow stays unproved. A synthetic whole-row
read cannot refute a projection-only column policy: column counterexamples
execute an authored observer in two schema-valid worlds differing only in that
column, with policy false in both. Outputs must differ reproducibly. Maintained
values are recomputed; column-sensitive counts/existence are included. Separate
obligations name unresolved raw-probe, wire, device and opaque flows. A direct
column theorem never silently clears those obligations. Per-column audit
reports effective audience plus operation/derived observers, not only its type.

### 12.2 Reproducible campaigns and reachability

Generated seed ingress contains only authored base rows/fields. Never re-seed
maintained targets or engine-recorded fields. Generated lifetime samples are
live at the campaign clock; principal fields vary independently. Rejected
snapshots are recorded as unsupported, not successful attempts. Existing
fixture histories instead fork the complete SQLite store and asset ledger into
owned isolated memory stores; captures, deadlines, maintained values, lifetimes
and actual media identities survive unchanged. No source store is modified.
Temporary asset directories belong to Store and disappear on drop.

Actors include all principal values in fixtures. Required/optional references
range over fixture rows, not only the first. Short histories visit first steps
breadth first before extending them under a reported work cap. Read and row
assertions are checked after each step. Write/operation assertions judge only
actual committed changes, correct insert/update/delete arms and pre-write
policy state; ambiguous multiple writes, implicit effects or unstable policy
need executed-effect tracing and remain unsupported. A finite trace is never
an induction proof for always or arbitrary histories.

Every counterexample has a machine-readable origin: authored trace from trusted
fixture, trusted-seed-only, or synthetic operation. Only the first establishes
application reachability from the recorded trusted starting state and fails
normal check/test. Seed/synthetic witnesses remain refutations of all-snapshot
claims, visible with --all and JSON, without failing acceptance. Unproved
reachability is never asserted unreachable as a mathematical theorem. Check
loads the same fixture base for reachable histories as test, without HTTP or
business-case execution. A stronger witness must replace a synthetic witness
for the same declaration. --all exposes all witnesses and never changes which
ones fail. No unreachable refutation may print pass/ok beside exit 1.

### 12.3 Actionable authority advice

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies authored caller operations that write role
or group grants trusted by later rules while the effective admission does not
establish independent authority to confer that particular grant. Include
self-signup roles and transitions where a caller can confer a role they lack.
A trusted service seat or an independently authorized administrator may appoint
other roles; holding an unrelated ordinary role does not establish delegation.
Advice names the operation, grant and gate and points to guide staging's
operator first-admin recipe. It is advice, never an implicit runtime refusal
or a proof of malicious intent. Legitimate open enrollment can be intentional.

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE requires an actually mutable/revocable current-role
population and a retained principal/grant branch lacking that role recheck;
cover read AND write branches. Immutable/static role populations do not fire.
N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE excludes public populations and caller-owned role lookups;
only an absent/hidden distinction crossing a narrower private audience warrants
advice. Every security note has a real source location and contributes to the
reported note count. Rule printing preserves connective precedence. Identity
429 responses include a conservative retry delay covering the rate window;
the existing limits and authentication semantics are unchanged.

### 12.4 Evidence

Qualification uses read-only copies of all ten wave-17 high lanes and their
spec-authored invariant sets, plus all 150 earlier schemas. Report caught,
partial and not caught separately; all-snapshot witnesses alone do not count
as a reachable detection. Preserve bad/fixed controls, note counts and source
hashes. The open qualification-gap ticket remains open for unresolved whole
proof/device/opaque obligations. No zero-hole claim follows from finite tests.


### 12.5 SEC3 reduction details

The direct column theorem is `(row_read AND owner_private_gate) => P` in the
existing three-valued kernel; deny-delete is never an admission for column
read. Whole proof still requires every observer obligation. This increment
uses dynamic column-world observers, not an unimplemented static dataflow
claim: pure projections can avoid a refutation, but do not acquire a proof by
absence of finite counterexamples. Census is existing point/scan/view/filter/
count/sum/exists, mutation return/refusal/copy, asset, sync, raw/derived/opaque
paths. Unsupported field flow remains unproved. Audit's `column_audiences`
maps every visible column name to its effective row-and-private-field rule;
operations and derived edges remain separately enumerated.

Column worlds use the same ordinary viewer, server clock and row id; the named
column has two different schema-valid typed values, and policy is false on its
respective image in each committed world. Every non-native authored query and
deterministic mutation is eligible; fresh ids, effects and incomplete/resource
results are declined. Compare typed logical data and authored refusal messages,
retain ordered arrays, repeat both observations to reject unstable outputs.
Rebuild maintained rows from authored inputs, never accept fabricated derived
or recorded fields. Required media substitutions without a coupled real ledger
remain explicitly unsupported. A primary observation from the exact trusted
fixture is a reachable observation; its synthetic alternate world establishes
value dependency, not reachability of an alternate application history. JSON
retains both worlds and explicitly states this distinction.

`AuthoredFixtureTrace`, `TrustedSeedOnly`, `SyntheticOperation` are the witness
origins (serialized in kebab-case). Status remains refuted for an all-snapshot
counterexample. Normal text labels seed/synthetic refutations non-failing and
hides their large witnesses; --all reveals them and does not change exit status.
Only authored fixture observations fail check/test. A reachable witness wins
over a synthetic witness for the same declaration. With no fixture, check uses
schema-only exploration and names missing application-reachability coverage.
Check discovers an explicit --fixture or project fixtures/; it uses an isolated
in-memory loader at the fixed campaign clock, never the development store.

Candidates sort by operation, argument value and principal. Enumerate bounded
Cartesian arguments including nullable enums and fixture references; interleave
operations for breadth-first first steps, then diagonal ordered pairs. The
512 first-step/512-pair and 1000-fixture-row caps are reported; no exhaustive
claim is made for unvisited combinations. Fixture asset directories are owned
by Store and removed on drop, including error returns. Histories judge direct
single-write net images only; multiple writes, dependency-changing and opaque
boundaries remain unsupported rather than fabricating write-boundary events.

The escalation advisory compares role privileges structurally, never by an
administrator role-name allowlist. Enumerate literals from a role enum or
role probes over a text roster; substitute each caller/assigned role into the
roster write gates and other table-effect gates. A possible ordinary assignment
that adds an effect the caller lacks warrants advice; explicit service-only
branches are excluded from this advisory cast, not from invariant proofs.
Literal assignment targets restrict the target set; dynamic values retain all
candidate roles. This is overapproximate advice: independent operation guards,
intentional enrollment and delegation still need specification review.
Grant-lifetime notes require a role/kind column with an authored update/upsert/
delete and a mutable role field; immutable seed-only populations are excluded.
Existence advice requires a private table lookup, an independent stricter read
policy and distinct later refusal; public and viewer-owned lookups are excluded.
Locations are exact column/rule sites where available, table sites otherwise;
the project-wide missing-intent note uses the first source's line 1.

Identity rate refusals on the existing identity routes use the existing
60-second WINDOW: Retry-After: 60, denied.retryAfterMs: 60000. This is a
conservative wait, not exposure of individual rate-key state. No limit changes.
Qualification inputs are ~/sb4-loop/wave17-repro/sec/<lane>/snapback/security.q
and read-only wave17 app copies; the report pins their hashes. The ten high
lanes are 006/011/013/015/019/021/027/040/045/048. Caught means an authored
fixture observation of the reported bad behavior; partial retains a relevant
note or synthetic witness with a missing affected path. Each of the eight real
highs is scored separately. All eight caught and no false refusals on 150 older
schemas is the target, never inferred from an incomplete run.


### 12.6 Disclosure and clock clarifications

A column observer mutation is declined if it can change the protected image,
policy dependency or an unresolved implicit writer before disclosure. A
pre-operation false policy cannot refute an authorized post-transfer return.
Column observers also run after committed history steps, preserving the
trusted initial fixture and authored prefix. All reference and principal
arguments use the same bounded fixture Cartesian cases; truncation of read
observer cases at 32 is named. Clock-only whole-row probes exclude columns.
Column tick coverage beyond the recorded campaign clock remains named.

All security fixture loading, probes, writes, paired deletions and policy
judgments use the same explicit campaign clock. Business tests keep their own
existing clock and store; security reloads trusted fixture files independently.
Raw policy probes respect both not-before and expire-at bounds. Direct delete
assertions judge removed pre-images against the stable pre-write state, never
surviving rows alone; ambiguous implicit removals remain unsupported.

Fixture forks copy already activated engine state without re-adopting a schema.
They use fresh entropy/minter and private assets, never shared live connections.
Ephemeral-hub fixtures require a separate isolated snapshot and are declined.
Structural privilege advice combines dominating safe operation requirements
with the actual insert/update/upsert gate and applicable owned-field patch gate.
A mutable roster alone is not enough for lifetime advice: require independent
current-role intent or a retained recipient column tied to a role literal
(judge, inspector, host, contractor, engineer; ordinary author attribution alone
is not a current-role contract). Deletion revokes even an immutable role field.

### 12.7 Stable judgments and direct payload witnesses

Unknown bound policy dependencies never authorize a mutation observer: such
column mutations require disclosure-boundary tracing. Pre-write assertions use
a transitive closure of maintained and cascade/set-null writes; a policy
dependency or target that may change implicitly makes the assertion unsupported.
The same rule covers insert, update, upsert and delete. Looped/opaque net images
cannot stand in for executed write events. Effective inserted authority includes
a defaulted role even when omitted from the source write.

An authored complete query returning a compiler-typed Row/Table or Page/Table
containing the protected row id is a direct payload witness at that query's
snapshot. Nested typed row returns retain their source-table identity. This
avoids inventing an invalid absent world by deleting a referenced parent.
Projected scalar/aggregate/refusal observations still require validated paired
worlds; a matching untyped id or value alone cannot attribute an exposure.

Check prioritizes actual fixture histories and column observers before searching
remaining synthetic counterexamples. It does not repeat the test-only operation
exploration matrix, which judges no additional policy. That matrix shares an
8192-call budget across every operation (1–512 calls per operation), reports
truncation, and preserves enum/reference/principal alternatives within that cap.
Required image/file operation arguments are actual uploads owned by the acting
principal, with ledger/scratch custody in the isolated engine. Unsupported video
and uncoupled column-world media identities remain named.

For escalation advice, a role-protected appointer is an explicit delegation
boundary; conferring a different role is not by itself evidence of escalation.
Independent role-value intent may restrict that boundary (for example a producer
must not confer legal). Ordinary role casts make service-seat equality false,
including inside operation requirements. Fixed literals still undergo the privilege comparison; ordinary enrollment
whose rules add no role-specific authority is not advised. Analyze caller-chosen
roles, defaulted inserted authority and owner-field upgrades; respect the
argument's actual enum choices. A fixed bootstrap that can appoint further
roles remains an actionable first-admin risk. Notes remain advisory; absence
of independent intent is not proof that a delegation matches the specification.

### 12.8 Runtime intent and independent worlds
