### 12.8 Runtime intent and independent worlds

Reference-hop intent is judged by the engine's own authorized RuleProbe and
three-valued verdict on an isolated actual snapshot. Hidden/missing references
remain Unknown, never false; snapshot-only evaluators decline those terms.
Temporal reference policies require a per-tick authorized replay and remain
explicitly unsupported until that path runs. Referenced immutable-column worlds
use fresh stores with copied verified asset ledgers and all authored base rows,
then ordinary seed validation/recomputed maintenance. They do not require a
legal delete transition. Every non-protected base field, capture, row identity
and row population must match before comparing observations.

Implicit stability includes source and member writes to row-keyed views,
maintained owner columns, through-parent effects and transitive cascade/set-null.
Only an actual direct delete can refute delete authority; commit-end expiry is
not relabeled a deletion by the caller. Defaulted upsert roles are analyzed on
the insert arm only; omitted fields are preserved on update. Revocability advice
requires an expanded write to a mutable role field or an admitted deletion,
not an unrelated display-name update.

For a write history, a stable necessary policy component may establish a
violation despite another changing conjunct. Replace unstable atoms (or an
entire unstable negation) by Allow, retaining And/Or composition; separately
verify `original => weakened` with the existing exhaustive kernel. Denial of
that weakened condition establishes denial of the original, never its proof.
Stability includes transitive read-authority dependencies of referenced rows.
Do not use this reduction when implicit writes can change the target image or
executed arm. This catches a demoted recorder whose role stays false even when
another statement updates an unrelated field on its parent. No new axiom is
introduced. Id-column presence/count intent shares the row identity observer
path; arbitrary untyped matching ids do not establish a payload witness.

Independent intent binds authorized reference operands without installing a
runtime read gate; live-hop restrictions on executable read rules do not remove
those operands from intent. Probe-key/equality reference hops participate in
stability and transitive authority dependency collection. Every committed
history state rechecks row assertions, including unchanged rows whose related
policy rows changed; pre-existing unchanged seed violations stay supplemental.
Role advice expands helpers with lexical argument substitution and dominating
requirements, retaining the actual write source site. Constant defaults and
literal assignments use the same authority comparison. Optional-column pairs
vary both values and null presence to observe value-existence aggregates.

Mutation column observers include transitive read-authority dependencies and
implicit targets when establishing policy stability. Role probes normalize
unique-key components and extra equalities identically. Argument-domain
narrowing follows the resolved argument expression, never a coincidentally
same-named argument beside a literal assignment. An unconditional helper call
exports its guaranteed requirement prefix to subsequent caller writes;
conditional/loop-local requirements do not become caller assumptions.

Column observers first judge authorized intent on the validated primary world
once per actor. They share a 16384-call campaign budget (including repeatability
and declined worlds), with explicit remaining-world coverage when exhausted.
The budget cannot discharge an obligation or turn an unproved path into proof.

Only current-role intent establishes that retaining a grant is wrong; naming a
principal column after a role does not. Lifetime advice therefore requires an
independent current-role invariant as well as an authored revocation writer.
The project-wide missing-intent note identifies tables with no invariants; it
does not diagnose a missing current-role clause when other intent is present.
Without independent current-role intent, lifetime advice stays silent: authors
must declare that contract before the checker can distinguish intentional
retained access from a stale grant. Global role
advice requires a principal/role unique roster; per-resource context keys need
a separate delegation reduction. Delegation guards constrain the actual role
column of that roster, never an ordinary project/status lookup. Assignment
intent is selected by effect, with upsert arms judged separately. Initial row
assertion failures remain visible as TrustedSeedOnly, even without mutations.
The operation matrix uses a shared remaining-call counter and fair per-operation
quotas of 1–512; above 8192 eligible operations, unvisited names are explicit.

For fixed ordinary enrollment, row-scoped permissions alone do not warrant
escalation advice. Without contrary independent intent, a fixed assignment
requires new unconditional read/delete or roster authority to emit the note;
caller-chosen roles retain the broader effect comparison. This advisory is not
an all-operation reachability theorem; scoped-resource delegation and fixed
write-only privilege distinctions require independent intent/reductions.
The report keeps the shortest reachable witness. Campaigns can also retain
separate one- and two-step examples without replacing a shorter trace or
attributing a pre-existing disclosure to an unrelated mutation. Named-operation
intent covers all its target-table effects; table/column insert/update/delete
intent covers only that executed arm. Column prefilters and paired intent use
validated committed images; unsupported synthetic operation judgments are named.

History read observers first select compiler-typed row-return candidates or the
transitive maintained/through/raw-probe table closure for scalar observations.
They reserve at most 32768 authored read calls per campaign, including repeats;
remaining disclosures are explicit uncovered work. Mutation sequence replay
continues within its own bounds. Read-call exhaustion never grants a proof.

Tuple-key role tests use the same column-aware normalization in lifetime advice.
A scoped enum context is not a role qualifier; qualifying unique keys contain
one principal and actual role qualifiers only. Named-operation delegation intent
is honored for that caller operation. Initial fixture column observations run
before history prefixes and synthetic exploration; continuation shares the same
work counters so a later phase cannot evict a zero-step witness by consuming its
budget first. §12.8's independent-current-role requirement supersedes the older
name-based alternative in §12.6.

Fixed-role comparison retains any additional probe context as an opaque
predicate; a matching role alone cannot make a region-qualified read globally
unconditional. Lifetime advice excludes a retained branch that already implies
the declared policy, including explicit owner alternatives. Arbitrarily named
role qualifiers require unambiguous literal-probe evidence before a compound
principal/qualifier key can be treated as a global roster.

Synthetic enumeration deduplicates identical candidate seed rows and typed
operation arguments after dependency pruning and literal pinning. Unrelated
schema enum digits cannot cause repeated replay of the same input. This is
finite input exploration, with no exhaustive entropy/generated-id claim.

A different or negated current-role check does not discharge retained-grant
intent: only implication of the applicable independent policy suppresses that
advice. Live roster probes are not themselves described as retained grants.
Repeated risky branches within one table/effect produce one located advisory;
audit retains their individual audiences and writers.

An initial column pass visits every sampled actor within its shared budget,
retaining each actor's first disclosure instead of stopping after the first
viewer. Later unrelated prefixes cannot replace an already observed zero-step
exposure for another sampled viewer.

Unprojected compiler-typed Row/Page payloads also witness column reads when the
returned field equals that committed physical value and its field-visibility
gate admits this viewer. This covers real media ids without manufacturing an
alternate asset ledger. Projected scalar/shape values retain the paired-world
requirement; a coincidentally matching constant is not column provenance. A
private field's redacted null is never treated as disclosure of a null value.

Absent contrary independent intent, caller-chosen role advice requires new
unconditional table authority; an additional trusted resource/ownership grant
is a scoped delegation boundary requiring its own reduction. Fixed enrollment
keeps the narrower read/delete/roster comparison. This avoids treating ordinary
self-enrollment for one's own records as privileged provisioning.

Principal-only global membership grants also participate in advice. An
unqualified viewer probe of a required unique principal denotes current
membership; authored deletion/principal reassignment can revoke it. Membership
creation receives escalation advice when it contradicts a pure caller-based
independent provisioning invariant (table insert/update/write or named operation).
Principal reassignment uses update authority including private-field ownership;
upsert creation and reassignment are judged on their separate arms. A dominating
global appointer role may reside in a separate roster, with all its qualifiers
retained when checking implication.
Ordinary account enrollment without such provisioning intent is not guessed to
be an administrator grant. Scoped/qualified membership still needs its reduction.

Paired query observations tag successful data separately from authored logical
refusals, retaining refusal codes and messages. Only engine-provided authored
rule/constraint markers qualify; incomplete reads, resource exhaustion and
ambiguous engine errors remain outside logical comparison. This prevents both
missed refusal oracles and collisions between a payload shape and a refusal.
Synthetic principal sampling separates the focal row's attribution from the
supporting roster/assignment recipient; finite enumeration still never proves
that all tuples are covered.

Within the unchanged 512-pair cap, up to 256 pairs prioritize a global-role
mutation followed by a distinct operation as the named affected principal.
Ref arguments resolve through fixture roster rows; scalar principal arguments
supply other recipients. First steps and successor operation names interleave;
remaining slots retain diagonal pairs. These are candidates only: real commits,
authorized policies and stable necessary conditions still decide witnesses.

The 16384 column budget counts call/world reservations: each world variant,
authority-judgment actor, visited observer program and generated snapshot reserves
a slot even when it declines before execution; each paired experiment reserves
four calls for primary, alternative and repeats. Typed history payload witnesses
reserve two read calls and require identical complete data on the committed image.
A failed repeat is named unsupported, never a reachable failing counterexample.

Write-arm predecessor matching uses physical unexpired rows, including rows
whose `not before` time is pending; read visibility never converts an update
into insert or hides an executed delete. Every generated and fixture column pair
compares both committed readbacks for non-protected base fields/captures and
populations, and records the actual committed alternate world. Repeatability
also checks those input worlds, not just the returned logical observations.

Whole-row paired observers use typed fixture argument products (32 per observer),
including both synthetic principals independently of the row's owner; state and
argument variants must not accidentally force every aggregate lookup to the
opposite owner. A shared 8192-call reservation budget covers primary/alternate
and repeats; exhaustion explicitly names the remaining uncovered worlds.

Column comparison also requires identical materialized observer arguments across
both worlds and repeats. Independently generated asset-input identities are an
explicit coupled-ledger blocker, not evidence about the protected column.

Time arguments sample one day before the campaign clock, the clock itself and
one day after; this controls input values, not STAGE/MIG's host clock. Singles
also retain successful unique candidates that populate an initially empty
read-protected media table and those that change an existing global role.
Up to 256 pair slots replay media creation then a role change, including changes
by another caller; actual uploaded media and both commits remain in the trace.
Remaining pair slots prefer the role-successor plan above, then diagonal pairs.
The 512-single/512-pair limits do not increase. This exercises stale media grants
without inventing asset-bearing seed rows or silently claiming longer histories.

History read observers materialize actor-owned image/file inputs before querying,
reuse those exact inputs for repeats, and clone the verified ledger into paired
absence worlds. Upload preparation failures and every failed typed/paired repeat
name their operation/invariant coverage blocker; they never silently clear an
obligation. Column pairs still decline independently materialized asset inputs
when their identities differ, as required by their fixed-input comparison.

Authority advice conjoins all supplied owned-field old-owner gates; it does not
OR ownership with the table gate. Only a statically exclusive owned-field patch
may bypass an unnamed update rule. Mixed patches and named transitions retain
the judged table gate, including private fields it needs to inspect. Insert
ownership applies to supplied fields, not omitted defaults. Named next-image
role literals participate in target-role substitution. The same calculation
governs role assignments and principal-only membership reassignment.

An update gate that implies the role/principal is unchanged is not an escalation
witness, even when its source patch explicitly supplies that field. Runtime
regressions cover owned-only, mixed, named-deny, named-no-change and named-target
restrictions for role writes, plus owned principal-only membership reassignment.

A validated empty fixture/store is also a trusted initial state. Histories do
not switch off merely because no authored rows exist: ordinary operations may
create data and subsequent authored queries may disclose it. Such traces retain
their real mutation history and authored-fixture origin. A query-only empty app
still cannot turn arbitrary seeded/synthetic rows into reachable failures. The
packed security-card proof executes its writers from empty state and requires
row and column disclosures to carry that reachable origin when the rule widens.

//! CLI security reporting; compile/test evidence share one analyzer.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#5-exposure-report — full audit is opt-in, intent notes are on ordinary check.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::security::{self,Report};
mod http;
pub(super) fn unauthenticated(bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)])->bool {
    match http::run(bound,sources){Ok(_)=>true,Err(why)=>{eprintln!("security HTTP failure: {why}");false}}
}
pub(super) fn report(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,options:&Options)->Report{
    let mut report=security::analyze(bound);
    if let Ok(sources)=project.sources(){security::add_source_notes(&mut report,&sources,bound);}
    let mut campaign=security::challenges::Campaign::default();
    if !bound.invariants.is_empty(){
        let mut selection=options.clone();
        if !selection.flags.contains_key("fixture")&&project.root.join("fixtures").is_dir(){selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),project.root.join("fixtures").to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
        let fixture=if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
            super::test_fixture::engine_at(project,bound,&selection,1_800_000_000_000)
        }else{
            campaign.unsupported.insert("no supplied fixture: histories start at the validated empty store; populated production states remain uncovered".into());
            snapback4_core::Store::memory().and_then(|store|snapback4_core::Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),1_800_000_000_000)).map_err(|why|why.to_string())
        };
        match fixture{
            Ok(fixture)=>security::challenges::fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,&mut campaign),
            Err(why)=>for verdict in &mut report.invariants{verdict.reasons.push(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));},
        }
    }
    let sites=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&!campaign.violations.iter().any(|c|c.invariant_site==v.invariant.site.to_string()&&c.reachability==security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace)).map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect();
    let campaign=security::challenges::run_selected_after(bound,Some(&sites),campaign);
    security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&campaign);report
}
pub fn audit(options:&Options)->i32{
    let project=project_or_exit();
    let bound=compiled_or_exit(match project.compile(){Ok(b)=>b,Err(e)=>{eprintln!("audit: {e}");return 2;}},options.json);
    let report=report(&project,&bound,options);
    if options.json {println!("{}",serde_json::to_string(&report).unwrap());}
    else {print!("{}",report.text_with_all(options.flags.contains_key("all")));}
    if report.refuted(){1}else{0}
}
pub(super) fn summary(report:&Report,all:bool){
    if let Some(coverage)=report.campaign_coverage.iter().find(|coverage|coverage.starts_with("no supplied fixture:")){eprintln!("security not covered: {coverage}");}
    for v in &report.invariants {if !all&&v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability!=security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace){eprintln!("security: {} refuted by seed/synthetic witness only (non-failing; --all for detail)",v.invariant.target_label());continue;}eprintln!("security {:?}: {} {}",v.status,v.invariant.target_label(),v.invariant.predicate);for why in &v.reasons{eprintln!("  {why}");}if let Some(c)=&v.counterexample{eprintln!("  counterexample {}",serde_json::to_string(c).unwrap());}}
    for note in &report.notes{eprintln!("{} {}: {} — {}",note.site,note.code,note.message,note.rewrite);}
    if !report.invariants.is_empty(){eprintln!("security: audit --json lists paths, exclusions and grant writers; guide security explains proof scope");}
}

//! Finite three-valued implication, deliberately independent of database facts.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#3-sound-proof-boundary — impossible valuations overapproximate all snapshots.
use snapback4_core::schema::{RuleExpr as R, RuleTerm as T};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

pub const MAX_ATOMS: usize = 10;
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Truth { Yes, No, Unknown }
impl Truth {
    fn not(self) -> Self { match self { Self::Yes => Self::No, Self::No => Self::Yes, Self::Unknown => Self::Unknown } }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, and: bool) -> Self {
        let decisive = if and { Self::No } else { Self::Yes };
        if self == decisive || other == decisive { decisive }
        else if self == Self::Unknown || other == Self::Unknown { Self::Unknown }
        else { decisive.not() }
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum Formula { Constant(Truth), Atom(usize), Not(Box<Self>), And(Vec<Self>), Or(Vec<Self>) }
impl Formula {
    fn eval(&self, values: &[Truth]) -> Truth {
        match self {
            Self::Constant(v) => *v, Self::Atom(n) => values[*n], Self::Not(x) => x.eval(values).not(),
            Self::And(xs) => xs.iter().fold(Truth::Yes, |v, x| v.combine(x.eval(values), true)),
            Self::Or(xs) => xs.iter().fold(Truth::No, |v, x| v.combine(x.eval(values), false)),
        }
    }
}
fn term(t: &T) -> T { match t { T::NextField(f) => T::Field(f.clone()), other => other.clone() } }
fn atom(rule: &R) -> String {
    let mut r = rule.clone();
    match &mut r {
        R::Eq(a,b) | R::Compare(_,a,b) => { *a=term(a); *b=term(b); }
        R::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | R::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
            for t in key { *t=term(t); }
            for (_,t) in equalities.iter_mut() { *t=term(t); }
            equalities.sort_by(|a,b| a.0.cmp(&b.0));
        }
        _ => {}
    }
    if let R::Eq(a,b)=&mut r && serde_json::to_string(a).unwrap()>serde_json::to_string(b).unwrap() { std::mem::swap(a,b); }
    let mut json=serde_json::to_value(&r).unwrap();
    // Provenance controls planning, not rule evaluation. Remove only this
    // leaf's metadata, never a similarly named literal-object field.
    for name in ["Exists","Created"] { if let Some(body)=json.get_mut(name).and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object_mut) { body.remove("provenance"); } }
    json.to_string()
}
fn formula(rule: &R, atoms: &mut Vec<String>, nodes: &mut usize) -> Result<Formula, String> {
    *nodes+=1; if *nodes>1024 { return Err("formula exceeds 1024 nodes".into()); }
    Ok(match rule {
        R::Allow | R::Public {..} => Formula::Constant(Truth::Yes), R::Deny => Formula::Constant(Truth::No),
        R::And(xs) => Formula::And(xs.iter().map(|r| formula(r,atoms,nodes)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        R::Or(xs) => Formula::Or(xs.iter().map(|r| formula(r,atoms,nodes)).collect::<Result<_,_>>()?),
        R::Not(x) => Formula::Not(Box::new(formula(x,atoms,nodes)?)),
        R::Ne(a,b) => Formula::Not(Box::new(formula(&R::Eq(a.clone(),b.clone()),atoms,nodes)?)),
        R::Eq(T::Literal(a),T::Literal(b)) => Formula::Constant(if a==b {Truth::Yes}else{Truth::No}),
        R::Eq(T::Viewer,T::Viewer) => Formula::Constant(Truth::Yes),
        other => { let key=atom(other); let n=atoms.iter().position(|a| a==&key).unwrap_or_else(|| {atoms.push(key);atoms.len()-1});
            if atoms.len()>MAX_ATOMS {return Err(format!("more than {MAX_ATOMS} independent predicates"));} Formula::Atom(n) }
    })
}
/// `Ok(true)` is a proof; `Ok(false)` is only an abstract countermodel.
/// Neither limit exhaustion nor a countermodel is a concrete refutation.
pub fn implies(admission: &R, policy: &R) -> Result<bool,String> {
    let (mut atoms,mut nodes)=(Vec::new(),0);
    let a=formula(admission,&mut atoms,&mut nodes)?;
    let p=formula(policy,&mut atoms,&mut nodes)?;
    let mut values=vec![Truth::Unknown;atoms.len()];
    for mut n in 0..3usize.pow(atoms.len() as u32) {
        for v in &mut values { *v=match n%3 {0=>Truth::Yes,1=>Truth::No,_=>Truth::Unknown}; n/=3; }
        if a.eval(&values)==Truth::Yes && p.eval(&values)!=Truth::Yes { return Ok(false); }
    }
    Ok(true)
}
pub fn dependencies(rule: &R) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    let mut out=BTreeSet::new();
    match rule {
        R::Exists{table,key,equalities,..}|R::Created{table,key,equalities,..} => {out.insert(table.clone());for term in key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)){for (table,_) in term.references(){out.insert(table.into());}}}
        R::And(xs)|R::Or(xs) => for x in xs {out.extend(dependencies(x));}, R::Not(x) => out.extend(dependencies(x)),
        R::Eq(a,b)|R::Ne(a,b)|R::Compare(_,a,b) => for t in [a,b] { for (table,_) in t.references() {out.insert(table.into());} },
        _=>{}
    }
    out
}
#[cfg(test)] mod tests {
    use super::*;
    fn a() -> R { R::Eq(T::Field("owner".into()),T::Viewer) }
    #[test] fn kleene_unknown_is_neither_true_nor_false() {
        assert_eq!(Truth::Unknown.not(),Truth::Unknown);
        assert_eq!(Truth::No.combine(Truth::Unknown,true),Truth::No);
        assert_eq!(Truth::Yes.combine(Truth::Unknown,false),Truth::Yes);
        assert!(!implies(&R::Allow,&R::Or(vec![a(),R::Not(Box::new(a()))])).unwrap());
    }
    #[test] fn no_boolean_assignment_is_a_concrete_refutation() {
        assert!(implies(&R::And(vec![a(),R::Deny]),&R::Deny).unwrap());
        assert!(!implies(&R::Allow,&a()).unwrap());
        assert!(implies(&a(),&R::Eq(T::Viewer,T::Field("owner".into()))).unwrap());
        let xs=(0..11).map(|n|R::Eq(T::Field(format!("f{n}")),T::Viewer)).collect();
        assert!(implies(&R::And(xs),&a()).is_err());
    }
}
//! Concrete, isolated challenges; finite campaigns never establish proof.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#4-counterexamples-and-generated-tests — only replayed admission is a refutation.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{Engine,Store,Context,Value,RuntimeError};
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType as C,Constraint};
use snapback4_core::engine::rules::{self,Probe};
use snapback4_core::ir::{self,Expr,Stmt,Program,ProgramKind,Read,KeyProvenance};
pub const NOW:i64=1_800_000_000_000;
const VIEWER:&str="dev:security-a";
const OTHER:&str="dev:security-b";
// Same verified 1x1 PNG header used by the existing packaged asset witnesses.
const PNG:[u8;24]=[137,80,78,71,13,10,26,10,0,0,0,13,73,72,68,82,0,0,0,1,0,0,0,1];
mod paired;
mod timed;
mod histories;
mod assets;
mod column_reads;
mod stability;
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize,PartialEq,Eq)]
#[serde(rename_all="kebab-case")]
pub enum Reachability { AuthoredFixtureTrace, TrustedSeedOnly, SyntheticOperation }
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Counterexample {pub reachability:Reachability,pub invariant_site:String,pub args:BTreeMap<String,Value>,pub viewer:String,pub operation:String,pub effect:String,pub row:Value,pub old:Option<Value>,pub supporting_rows:BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,pub rule_path:String,pub scope:String, pub observations:Option<paired::Observations>,pub history:Vec<histories::Step>}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Default,Serialize)]
pub struct Campaign {pub paired_observer_calls:usize,pub history_observer_calls:usize,pub column_observer_calls:usize,pub operations:BTreeMap<String,usize>,pub sequences:usize,pub attempted:usize,pub admitted:usize,pub refused:usize,pub violations:Vec<Counterexample>,pub boundary_failures:Vec<String>,pub unsupported:BTreeSet<String>}
fn id(table:&str)->Value {Value::Id(format!("security-{table}"))}
fn sample(kind:&C,variant:usize)->Option<Value>{Some(match kind{
    C::Optional(inner)=>if variant&4!=0{sample(inner,variant).unwrap_or(Value::Null)}else{Value::Null},C::Id|C::Principal=>Value::Id(if variant&1==0{VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into()),
    C::Ref{table}=>id(table),C::Text{max}=>Value::String("x".repeat((*max).min(20) as usize)),
    C::Enum{variants}=>Value::String(variants.get(variant%variants.len())?.clone()),
    C::Int|C::Decimal{..}|C::Money{..}=>Value::Int(1),C::IntRange{min,max}=>Value::Int(1i64.clamp(*min,*max)),
    C::Bool=>Value::Bool((variant>>2)&1==0),C::Time=>Value::Int(NOW-1000),C::Json{..}=>Value::Null,C::Bytes{..}=>Value::Bytes(Vec::new()),
    C::List{..}=>Value::Array(Vec::new()),C::ListRange{of,min,..}=>Value::Array((0..*min).map(|_|sample(of,variant)).collect::<Option<_>>()?),
    C::Image{..}=>Value::String("$security:image".into()),C::File{..}=>Value::String("$security:file".into()),C::Video{..}=>return None,
})}
fn needed(schema:&Schema,name:&str,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){
    if !out.insert(name.into()){return;}let Some(t)=schema.table(name)else{return};
    for m in schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.target==name){needed(schema,&m.source,out);}
    for k in t.columns.values(){if let C::Ref{table}=k.required(){needed(schema,table,out);}}
    for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){needed(schema,&dep,out);}}
}
fn snapshot(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    snapshot_mode(bound,inv,variant,false)
}
fn snapshot_mode(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize,timed:bool)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    let mut names=BTreeSet::new();needed(&bound.schema,&inv.table,&mut names);
    if let Some(policy)=&inv.policy{for dep in kernel::dependencies(policy){needed(&bound.schema,&dep,&mut names);}}
    if let Some(mut syntax)=inv.syntax.clone(){super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut syntax,&mut |e| {
        if let ast::Expr::Read(r)|ast::Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=e {needed(&bound.schema,&r.table,&mut names);}
    });}
    if let Some(op)=&inv.operation && let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op){for kind in p.program.args.values(){if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind{needed(&bound.schema,table,&mut names);}}}
    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();
    let mut enum_case=variant;
    for name in names {
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&name];
        if bound.schema.maintain_for(&name).is_some(){continue;}
        let mut fields=BTreeMap::new();fields.insert("id".into(),id(&name));
        let mut principal_index=0usize;
        for (n,k) in &t.columns{
            if n=="id"||t.engine_field(n)||t.maintained.contains_key(n){continue;}
            let sample_variant=if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){let n=enum_case % variants.len();enum_case/=variants.len();n}else if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal){let n=((variant>>((if name==inv.table{0}else{3})+principal_index.min(5)))&1)|(variant&4);principal_index+=1;n}else{variant};
            let value=sample(k,sample_variant)
                .ok_or_else(||format!("{name}.{n}: asset ledger/required sample is not available"))?;
            fields.insert(n.clone(),value);
        }
        if timed || t.read_lifetime_sensitive() {for (n,k) in &t.columns {if matches!(k.required(),C::Time)&&(!k.is_optional()||variant&4!=0) {
            fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_ref()==Some(n){2000}else if timed{1000}else{-1000}));
        }}}
        for constraint in &t.constraints{if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=constraint
            && matches!(fields.get(field),Some(Value::String(_))) {fields.insert(field.clone(),Value::String("x".repeat((*min).max(1) as usize)));}}
        // Named primary keys used by role probes must select the challenged
        // viewer; changing the enum represents a current-role change while
        // derived assignment/membership rows remain present.
        if name!=inv.table {for (i,(n,k)) in t.columns.iter().filter(|(_,k)|matches!(k,C::Principal)).enumerate(){fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Id(if variant & (1 << (i+3)) == 0 {VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into())); let _=k;}}
        out.insert(name,vec![Value::Row(fields.into())]);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
struct Snapshot<'a>{schema:&'a Schema,rows:&'a BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>}
impl Probe for Snapshot<'_>{
    fn exists(&mut self,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[Value],equalities:&[(String,Value)])->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
        let t=&self.schema.tables[table];let Some(idx)=t.index(index)else{return Ok(false)};
        if idx.components.len()!=key.len(){return Ok(false);}
        if idx.unique&&idx.components.iter().any(|c|c.column().is_some_and(|n|t.columns.get(n).is_some_and(C::is_optional)))&&key.contains(&Value::Null){return Ok(false);}
        Ok(self.rows.get(table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|r|live(t,r)).any(|r|idx.components.iter().zip(key).all(|(c,v)|c.column().is_some_and(|n|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v)))&&equalities.iter().all(|(n,v)|r.field(n).ok()==Some(v))))
    }
    fn created(&mut self,_:&str,_:&str,_:&[Value])->bool{false}

}
fn live(t:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,row:&Value)->bool{
    !t.expired(row,NOW)&&t.not_before.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|match row.field(c){Ok(Value::Int(n))=>*n<=NOW,Ok(Value::Null)=>true,_=>false})
}
fn policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value)->Result<bool,String>{

    let Some(p)=inv.policy.as_ref() else{return inv.syntax.as_ref().and_then(|e|super::concrete::evaluate(e,row,rows,&bound.schema,viewer)).ok_or_else(||"concrete policy expression is unknown/unsupported (including clock and typed literal coercions)".into());};

    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(NOW));}
    if p.has_reference(){return Err("reference intent requires authorized engine policy replay".into());}
    rules::evaluate(p,viewer,old,&image,false,&mut Snapshot{schema:&bound.schema,rows}).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
fn policy_in(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,engine:&mut Engine)->Result<bool,String>{
    let Some(p)=&inv.policy else{return policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row);};
    if !p.has_reference(){return policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row);}
    let mut image=row.clone();if super::temporal::has_clock(p)&&let Value::Row(fields)=&mut image{fields.insert("$security:server-clock".into(),Value::Int(NOW));}
    match engine.fixture_policy(p,viewer,old,&image,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?{
        rules::Verdict::Readable=>Ok(true),rules::Verdict::Denied=>Ok(false),rules::Verdict::Unknown=>Err("policy reference/coverage judgment is unknown; never a refutation".into()),
    }
}
fn program(name:&str,kind:ProgramKind,body:Vec<Stmt>)->Program{Program{name:name.into(),kind,args:BTreeMap::new(),body,read_time_granularity_ms:None,returned_shapes:BTreeMap::new(),native:false}}
fn engine(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probe:Program)->Result<Engine,String>{
    engine_with(bound,rows,vec![probe])
}
fn engine_with(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probes:Vec<Program>)->Result<Engine,String>{
    engine_on_store(bound,rows,probes,Store::memory().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?)
}
fn engine_on_store(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,probes:Vec<Program>,store:Store)->Result<Engine,String>{
    let mut programs=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();programs.extend(probes);
    let mut engine=Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),programs,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    fn hydrate(engine:&mut Engine,kind:&C,value:&Value)->Result<Value,String>{
        match (kind,value) {
            (C::Optional(inner),v) if *v!=Value::Null=>hydrate(engine,inner,v),
            (C::List{of,..}|C::ListRange{of,..},Value::Array(xs))=>xs.iter().map(|v|hydrate(engine,of,v)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>().map(Value::Array),
            (C::Image{..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:image"=>engine.store_asset(VIEWER,&PNG,NOW).map(|a|a.column_value()).map_err(|e|format!("verified image fixture: {e}")),
            (C::File{types,..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:file"=>assets::file(engine,VIEWER,types),
            _=>Ok(value.clone()),
        }
    }
    let mut seeded=rows.iter().filter(|(name,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).map(|(name,rs)|{
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];
        let clean=rs.iter().map(|r|if let Value::Row(fields)=r {Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect())}else{r.clone()}).collect();
        (name.clone(),clean)
    }).collect::<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>();
    for (table,values) in &mut seeded {for row in values {for (column,kind) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns {
        if let Ok(v)=row.field(column) {
            let value=hydrate(&mut engine,kind,v)?;
            if let Value::Row(fields)=row {fields.insert(column.clone(),value);}
        }
    }}}
    engine.seed_at(seeded.iter().flat_map(|(t,rs)|rs.iter().map(move|r|(t.clone(),r.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>(),NOW).map_err(|e|format!("generated snapshot rejected by schema: {e}"))?;
    let actual=readback(&engine)?;
    for (name,values) in &actual {for row in values {
        snapback4_core::engine::validate::validate_row(&bound.schema,name,row).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        for c in &bound.schema.tables[name].constraints {if let Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{table,index,key}=c {
            let rule=RuleExpr::Exists{table:table.clone(),index:index.clone(),key:key.clone(),provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed,equalities:Vec::new()};
            if engine.fixture_policy(&rule,snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,None,row,NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=rules::Verdict::Readable {return Err(format!("{name}: trusted seed did not establish final-exists constraint"));}
        }}
    }}
    Ok(engine)
}
fn replay(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,word:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(bool,Value),String>{
    let name="$security.probe";let table=inv.table.clone();let key=id(&table);
    let read=Read{site:"security challenge".into(),table:table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(key.clone())],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed};
    let mut seeds=rows.clone();
    let probe=match word{
        "read"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(read))]),
        "insert"=>{seeds.remove(&table);program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Insert{table,row:Expr::Literal(row.clone())},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])},
        "delete"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Delete{table,key:Expr::Literal(key)},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]),
        "update"=>{
            let definition=&bound.schema.tables[&table];
            let changed=definition.columns.iter().filter(|(n,_)|n.as_str()!="id"&&!definition.engine_field(n))
                .filter(|(n,_)|inv.column.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|c==*n))
                .filter(|(n,_)|!definition.constraints.iter().any(|c|matches!(c,Constraint::Immutable{fields} if fields.contains(n))))
                .find_map(|(n,k)| {let old=row.field(n).ok()?;let next=match (k.required(),old){
                    (C::Bool,Value::Bool(v))=>Value::Bool(!v),
                    (C::Enum{variants},_)=>Value::String(variants.iter().find(|v|Some(Value::String((*v).clone()))!=Some(old.clone()))?.clone()),
                    (C::Text{max},Value::String(v)) if v.chars().count()<*max as usize=>Value::String(format!("{v}x")),
                    (C::Int|C::Time,Value::Int(v))=>Value::Int(v+1),_=>return None};
                    Some((n.clone(),next))});
            let Some((column,value))=changed else{return Err("no valid changed mutable-column sample".into());};
            program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Update{table,key:Expr::Literal(key),patch:Expr::Literal(Value::Map([(column,value)].into_iter().collect()))},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])
        },_=>return Err("row integrity/transition challenge is not an operation admission test".into()),
    };
    let mut engine=engine(bound,&seeds,probe)?;
    if word=="read"{return engine.query(name,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map(|q|(q.data!=Value::Null,row.clone())).map_err(|e|e.to_string());}
    let outcome=engine.mutate(name,None,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::new()).at(NOW)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    if !matches!(outcome.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..}){return Ok((false,row.clone()));}
    let next=engine.store().rows(&inv.table).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.into_iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
    if word=="delete"{return if next.is_none(){Ok((true,row.clone()))}else{Err("delete returned sent without removing the target".into())};}
    let next=next.ok_or("sent write has no committed target image")?;
    if word=="update" {

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'cat snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/testing.rs' in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b18-sec4
 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Saved-source fixture selection, business cases and the query sweep.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#9-wave-13-saved-version-proofs-day3-2026-10-16 — contemporary fixtures belong to the selected saved source.
use super::*;

const CASE_SUMMARY: &str = "SNAPBACK4_TEST_CASES";

fn case_count(path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<usize, String> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
    Ok(text.lines().filter(|line| {
        let line = line.trim();
        !line.is_empty() && !line.starts_with("//")
    }).count())
}

/// `test`: run every argument-free query as each persona against the store
/// (optionally loaded from a fixture) and print the meters beside the
/// ceilings, so a builder reads numbers instead of being refused on a bound.
pub fn test(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    let project = match proof_source::project(options, "source") { Ok(project) => project, Err(e) => { eprintln!("test: {e}"); return 2; } };
    let sources = match proof_source::sources(&project, options, "source") { Ok(sources) => sources, Err(e) => { eprintln!("test: {e}"); return 2; } };
    let bound = compiled_or_exit(proof_source::compile(&sources, options, "source"), options.json);
    let mut defaults = options.clone();
    let mut skipped_business_cases = 0;
    if !defaults.flags.contains_key("fixture") {
        let saved = options.flags.get("source").map(std::path::Path::new).map(|path| {
            if path.is_dir() { path.join("fixtures") }
            else if path.file_name().is_some_and(|name| name == "schema.q") { path.parent().unwrap().join("fixtures") }
            else { path.with_file_name(format!("{}-fixtures", path.file_stem().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy())) }
        });
        if let Some(fixture) = saved.filter(|path| path.is_dir()) {
            eprintln!("test: using {} (saved version's contemporary fixtures; override with --fixture <dir>)", fixture.display());
            defaults.flags.insert("fixture".into(), fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
        } else if project.root.join("fixtures").is_dir() {
            let fixture = project.root.join("fixtures");
            if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
                defaults.flags.insert("saved-fixture-fallback".into(), String::new());
                eprintln!("test: no contemporary fixtures for saved source; validating only declared-table files in {}; pass --fixture <dir> for a complete contemporary case suite", fixture.display());
                let cases = fixture.join(test_cases::FILE);
                if cases.is_file() {
                    skipped_business_cases = match case_count(&cases) { Ok(count) => count, Err(error) => { eprintln!("test: {error}"); return 1; } };
                    eprintln!("test: skipped {} — working cases may target another release; pass --fixture <dir> to run explicitly", cases.display());
                }
            } else { eprintln!("test: using {} (default fixture directory; override with --fixture <dir>)", fixture.display()); }
            defaults.flags.insert("fixture".into(), fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
        } else if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            eprintln!("test: no saved or working fixtures; query sweep only; pass --fixture <dir> for business cases");
        }
    }
    let options = &defaults;
    // test applies dev's cast: a persona dev would refuse is not run as an ordinary viewer.
    // Residency measures any principal in the data (scale fixtures name thousands), so it notes instead.
    if let Some(why) = options.flags.get("as").and_then(|names| names.split(',').find_map(|name| test_cases::undeclared_persona(name, &bound.personas))) {
        if !options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
            eprintln!("test --as: {why}");
            return 1;
        }
        eprintln!("test --residency --as: measuring an undeclared principal; dev would refuse it as a persona");
    }
    let _owner = if options.flags.contains_key("fixture") || options.flags.contains_key("source") { None } else {
        match crate::store_owner::command(&project.store_path()) {
            Ok(owner) => Some(owner),
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    };
    let fixture_now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    let opened = match &_owner {
        Some(owner) => owner.open_engine(&bound, std::time::Duration::from_secs(5), fixture_now).map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
        None => test_fixture::engine_at(&project, &bound, options, fixture_now),
    };
    let mut engine = match opened {
        Ok(engine) => engine,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
    };
    let personas = residency::personas(&bound, options);
    let sweep_personas = if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") { Default::default() } else {
        match test_sweep::load(options.flags.get("fixture"), &bound) {
            Ok(matrix) => matrix,
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    };
    if let Some(persona) = options.flags.get("as") && !sweep_personas.is_empty() {
        println!("test --as {persona} overrides test.sweep.json because --as requests one deliberate global probe; for: {}", sweep_personas.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
        let verbose = options.flags.contains_key("verbose");
        if let Err(error) = residency::measure(&mut engine, &bound, &personas, verbose) { eprintln!("residency measurement failed: {error}"); return 1; }
        if !verbose {
            if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
                println!("{CASE_SUMMARY} {{\"run\":0,\"skipped\":{skipped_business_cases}}}");
            }
            if !options.flags.contains_key("fixture-only") {
                eprintln!("test --residency executed no business cases; use --fixture-only for acquisition measurement, or omit --residency to run the cases");
                return 1;
            }
            return 0;
        }
    }
    let mut security_campaign = snapback4_lang::security::challenges::Campaign::default();
    if options.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
        match test_fixture::engine_at(&project,&bound,options,snapback4_lang::security::challenges::NOW){
            Ok(security_fixture)=>snapback4_lang::security::challenges::operations(&bound,&security_fixture,&mut security_campaign),
            Err(why)=>{security_campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("trusted fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
        }
    }else{
        security_campaign.unsupported.insert("no trusted fixture files for the fixed campaign clock; development store is not replayed".into());
        if let Ok(store)=snapback4_core::Store::memory(){
            match snapback4_core::Engine::new_at(store,bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),snapback4_lang::security::challenges::NOW){
                Ok(isolated)=>snapback4_lang::security::challenges::operations(&bound,&isolated,&mut security_campaign),
                Err(why)=>{security_campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("schema matrix unavailable: {why}"));}
            }
        }
    }
    let security_campaign=snapback4_lang::security::challenges::run_selected_after(&bound,None,security_campaign);
    let mut security_campaign=security_campaign;
    let security_http_ok=security::unauthenticated(&bound,&sources);
    if security_http_ok{security_campaign.unsupported.retain(|s|!s.starts_with("unauthenticated HTTP attempts require"));}
    println!("security histories: {} ordered short operation sequences replayed",security_campaign.sequences);
    for why in &security_campaign.boundary_failures {println!("security boundary failure: {why}");}
    println!("security operation matrix: {} operations exercised; unknown authorization intent is not inferred from success",security_campaign.operations.len());
    let reachable=security_campaign.violations.iter().filter(|c|c.reachability==snapback4_lang::security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace).count();
    println!("security tests: {} attempted, {} refused, {reachable} reachable violations; {} seed/synthetic observations (non-failing); finite campaign, not proof",security_campaign.attempted,security_campaign.refused,security_campaign.violations.len()-reachable);
    for c in security_campaign.violations.iter().filter(|c|options.flags.contains_key("all")||c.reachability==snapback4_lang::security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace) {println!("security violation: {}",serde_json::to_string(c).unwrap());}
    for reason in &security_campaign.unsupported {println!("security not covered: {reason}");}
    let (mut passed, mut failed_cases) = (0, 0);
    if let Some(fixture) = options.flags.get("fixture").filter(|_| !options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback")) {
        match test_cases::run_verbose(&mut engine, &bound, fixture, &sources, fixture_now, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")) {
            Ok((p, f)) => { passed = p; failed_cases = f; }
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    }
    let ceilings = engine.ceilings();
    let mut failures = 0;
    println!("test: {:<28} {:>8} {:>8} {:>8} {:>8} {:>10} {:>10}", "operation (persona)", "examined", "entries", "probes", "steps", "heap B", "output B");
    println!("      {:<28} {:>8} {:>8} {:>8} {:>8} {:>10} {:>10}", "ceilings", ceilings.examined, ceilings.index_entries, ceilings.rule_probes, ceilings.steps, ceilings.heap_bytes, ceilings.output_bytes);
    println!("legend: examined=rows visited; entries=index visits; probes=rule lookups; steps=interpreter work; heap B=charged heap; output B=encoded answer.");
    println!("verdicts: complete=whole answer; capped=partial; refused=expected-case or program-require refusal; denied=unexpected refusal; not run=no coverage; case OK=expectation passed.");
    let mut skipped = Vec::new();
    let mut held_not_run = 0;
    let mut near = Vec::new();
    let (mut complete, mut capped, mut refused_derived, mut expected) = (0usize, 0usize, 0usize, 0usize);
    let explicit = options.flags.get("fixture").filter(|_| !options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback")).map(|fixture| test_cases::explicit(fixture, &bound, fixture_now)).unwrap_or_default();
    for program in &bound.programs {
        if program.program.kind != ProgramKind::Query {
            continue;
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — a server fixture is not the device's held partition.
        if bound.schema.operations.get(&program.program.name).is_some_and(|fact| fact.held) {
            println!("      not run: {} is held (device only)", program.program.name);
            held_not_run += 1;
            continue;
        }
        let selected = if options.flags.contains_key("as") { &personas }
            else { sweep_personas.get(&program.program.name).unwrap_or(&personas) };
        for persona in selected {
            let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.clone() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
            let name = &program.program.name;
            let cases: Vec<&test_cases::Explicit> = explicit.iter().filter(|case| case.op == *name && case.principal == principal).collect();
            // Arguments: the persona's own case for this query when it has
            // one (the builder's choice, run as written); otherwise derived
            // from the data: a reference is a row of its table, preferring
            // one whose answer is not empty (the first row is often another
            // persona's, hidden), a principal is the persona, a text is the
            // shortest that fits. What cannot be derived is named, never
            // skipped silently.
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — wave 7: derived arguments tested nothing when the first row was hidden from the persona.
            let mut candidates: Vec<(BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::Value>, Option<&str>)> = Vec::new();
            if let Some(case) = cases.iter().find(|case| case.args.is_some()) {
                let decoded: Option<BTreeMap<_, _>> = case.args.as_ref().and_then(Json::as_object).and_then(|fields| fields.iter().map(|(arg, value)| {
                    let kind = program.program.args.get(arg)?;
                    wire::from_json(kind, value).ok().map(|value| (arg.clone(), value))
                }).collect());
                if let Some(args) = decoded { candidates.push((args, Some(case.at.as_str()))); }
            }
            let mut missing = Vec::new();
            if candidates.is_empty() {
                let rows = program.program.args.values().filter_map(|kind| match kind { ir::ArgType::Ref { table } => engine.store().rows(table).ok().map(|rows| rows.len()), _ => None }).max().unwrap_or(1).clamp(1, DERIVED_ROWS);
                for at in 0..rows {
                    let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
                    for (arg, kind) in &program.program.args {
                        match sample_arg(&engine, kind, &principal, at) {
                            Some(value) => { args.insert(arg.clone(), value); }
                            None if at == 0 => missing.push(format!("{arg}: {}", arg_word(kind))),
                            None => {}
                        }
                    }
                    if !missing.is_empty() { break; }
                    candidates.push((args, None));
                }
            }
            if !missing.is_empty() {
                skipped.push(format!("{name} ({persona}): needs {}", missing.join(", ")));
                continue;
            }
            // The first answer with rows wins; else the first answer; else the first refusal.
            let mut chosen: Option<(usize, BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::Value>, Option<&str>, Result<snapback4_core::QueryOutcome, snapback4_core::Refusal>, f64)> = None;
            let tried = candidates.len();
            for (args, source) in candidates {
                let started = Instant::now();
                let result = engine.query(name, Context::new(principal.clone(), args.clone()).at(fixture_now));
                let ms = started.elapsed().as_secs_f64() * 1000.0;
                let rank = match &result { Ok(outcome) if !empty_answer(&outcome.data) => 0, Ok(_) => 1, Err(_) => 2 };
                if chosen.as_ref().is_none_or(|(best, ..)| rank < *best) { chosen = Some((rank, args, source, result, ms)); }
                if rank == 0 || source.is_some() { break; }
            }
            let Some((rank, args, source, result, ms)) = chosen else { continue };
            let shown = if args.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!(" {}", serde_json::to_string(&args.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.clone(), wire::to_json(v))).collect::<serde_json::Map<_, _>>()).unwrap_or_default()) };
            let arguments = shown.clone();
            let shown = match source { Some(at) => format!("{shown} (from {at})"), None => shown };
            let derived = !args.is_empty() && source.is_none();
            let label = format!("{name} ({persona})");
            match result {
                Ok(outcome) => {
                    let m = outcome.metered;
                    let state = if outcome.complete { "complete" } else { "capped" };
                    if outcome.complete { complete += 1 } else { capped += 1 }
                    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.22.1 — report approach to a ceiling on the fixture, separately from actual cuts.
                    if outcome.capped_at.is_none() && outcome.word_cut_at.is_none() {
                        if let Some(cost) = crate::serve::read_cost::near_ceiling(m, ceilings) { near.push(format!("{label} {cost}")); }
                    }
                    let empty = if derived && rank == 1 && tried > 1 { format!(" — empty for each of {tried} derived rows: give it yours in {}", test_cases::FILE) } else { String::new() };
                    println!("      {label:<28} {:>8} {:>8} {:>8} {:>8} {:>10} {:>10}  {state} in {ms:.1} ms{shown}{empty}", m.examined, m.index_entries, m.rule_probes, m.steps, m.heap_bytes, m.output_bytes);
                }
                // A refusal the persona's own case expects is what the builder
                // asked for: say so instead of suggesting a case that exists.
                Err(refusal) if cases.iter().any(|case| case.expect.as_deref() == Some(refusal.code.as_str())) => {
                    expected += 1;
                    let at = cases.iter().find(|case| case.expect.as_deref() == Some(refusal.code.as_str())).map(|case| case.at.as_str()).unwrap_or_default();
                    println!("      {label:<28} refused {} as expected ({at}){}", refusal.code, if source == Some(at) { arguments } else { shown });
                }
                Err(refusal) if refusal.family == "rule" => {
                    // A program's own `require` refusing this persona is
                    // information, not a failure: the persona is not in that
                    // group. The caller chose nothing on this pass, whether
                    // the arguments were derived or there are none (wave 5
                    // lane 059: an argument-free gate counted as denied).
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — the derived pass never fails on the program's own require.
                    refused_derived += 1;
                    if let Some(at) = source {
                        println!("      {label:<28} refused {} on your arguments{shown} — the case at {at} runs below", refusal.code);
                    } else if derived {
                        let rows = if tried > 1 { format!(" (each of {tried} rows)") } else { String::new() };
                        println!("      {label:<28} refused {} on derived arguments{shown}{rows} — give it yours in {}: {{\"op\":\"{name}\",\"as\":\"{persona}\",\"args\":{{..}}}}", refusal.code, test_cases::FILE);
                    } else {
                        println!("      {label:<28} refused {} by its own require (not a failure: {persona} is not admitted) — pin it in {}: {{\"op\":\"{name}\",\"as\":\"{persona}\",\"expect\":\"{}\"}}", refusal.code, test_cases::FILE, refusal.code);
                    }
                }
                Err(refusal) => {
                    failures += 1;
                    println!("      {label:<28} denied {}: {}{shown}", refusal.code, refusal.message);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    for line in near.iter().take(10) { println!("near ceiling: {line}"); }
    if near.len() > 10 { println!("near ceiling: {} more query/persona reads passed half a ceiling without a cut", near.len() - 10); }
    for line in &skipped {
        println!("      not run: {line} — add a row of that table to the fixture, or a case in {}", test_cases::FILE);
    }
    // One line a script or an agent can read: derived refusals and unrun
    // queries are not failures, but they are not coverage either.
    let no_cases = passed + failed_cases == 0 && !options.flags.contains_key("fixture-only");
    let security_failed = security_campaign.violations.iter().any(|c|c.reachability==snapback4_lang::security::challenges::Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace) || !security_campaign.boundary_failures.is_empty() || !security_http_ok;
    let verdict = if failures > 0 || failed_cases > 0 || no_cases || security_failed { "FAIL" } else if !skipped.is_empty() || held_not_run > 0 { "incomplete" } else { "pass" };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — derived probe refusals are expected exploration, not failed cases.
    println!("test: {complete} complete, {capped} capped, {expected} refused as your cases expect, {refused_derived} probe refusals on derived arguments (expected), {} not run, {failures} denied{} — {verdict}",
        skipped.len() + held_not_run, if passed + failed_cases > 0 { format!("; cases: {passed} passed, {failed_cases} failed") } else if options.flags.contains_key("fixture") { format!("; no {} (see snapback4 guide testing)", test_cases::FILE) } else { String::new() });
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#11-wave-14-truthful-starter-custody-day4 — query-sweep reads are not application business cases.
    if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
        println!("{CASE_SUMMARY} {{\"run\":{},\"skipped\":{skipped_business_cases}}}", passed + failed_cases);
    }
    if no_cases {
        eprintln!("test: no business cases executed ({skipped_business_cases} skipped); add test.cases.jsonl, or use --fixture-only when fixture loading/query probes are the intended check");
    }
    if failures > 0 || failed_cases > 0 || no_cases || security_failed { 1 } else { 0 }
}

//! The same fixture loader serves query testing and residency at either CLI entry point.
use super::Options;
use crate::project::Project;
use snapback4_core::{Engine, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;

pub(super) fn engine(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    engine_at(project, bound, options, snapback4_core::now_ms())
}

pub(super) fn engine_at(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options, now: i64) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    let Some(fixture) = options.flags.get("fixture") else {
        if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            return Engine::new_at(store, bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|e| e.to_string());
        }
        return project.open_engine(bound).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open the store: {error}"));
    };
    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut schema = bound.schema.clone();
    // Residency defers ordinary maintains until after bulk loading and builds
    // them once; column derives stay installed and maintain as rows insert.
    let residency = options.flags.contains_key("residency");
    if residency { schema.maintains.retain(|m| m.private.is_some()); }
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut engine = Engine::new_at(store, schema, bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(fixture).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))? {
        let entry = entry.map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))?;
        names.push(entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string());
    }
    names.sort();
    // One batch for the whole directory: the engine orders parents before
    // children within a batch, so file order (and read_dir order) cannot make
    // a read-through child or a reference miss its parent.
    let mut rows = Vec::new();
    let mut unknown = Vec::new();
    for name in names {
        let Some(table) = name.strip_suffix(".jsonl") else { continue };
        if name == super::test_cases::FILE { continue; }
        let path = std::path::Path::new(fixture).join(&name);
        let Some(definition) = bound.schema.tables.get(table) else {
            if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") {
                eprintln!("test: skipped {} — saved source declares no table {table}; pass --fixture <dir> to select contemporary fixtures explicitly", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — the starter's own unmodified sample is skipped loudly; a project's file for a missing table still refuses.
            if !options.flags.contains_key("source") && super::init::starter_fixture(&name).is_some_and(|starter| std::fs::read_to_string(&path).is_ok_and(|text| text == starter)) {
                eprintln!("test: WARNING: skipped {} — the todo starter's unmodified sample rows for a `{table}` table your snapback/*.q does not declare; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's other leftovers", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            unknown.push(name);
            continue;
        };
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture file {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
        for (line_number, line) in text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty()) {
            let at = format!("{name}:{}", line_number + 1);
            let mut json: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
            // A residency fixture is often an exported store: engine-maintained
            // columns are dropped and rebuilt from the source rows. `test`
            // refuses them as an insert and `/seed` do (E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN).
            if residency && let Some(fields) = json.as_object_mut() { fields.retain(|field, _| !definition.maintained.contains_key(field)); }
            relative_times(definition, &mut json, now).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
            if let Some(why) = wire::bare_principal(definition, &json) { return Err(format!("{at}: {why}")); }
            let row = wire::row_from_json(definition, &json).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}\n  hint: a fixture row is one JSON object per line with the table's fields and an \"id\"; see snapback4 guide testing"))?;
            rows.push((table.to_owned(), row));
        }
    }
    // A misspelled file name would otherwise load nothing and still pass.
    if !unknown.is_empty() {
        let tables: Vec<&str> = bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
        // 0.4.17's init wrote the todo starter's rows beside a project's own schema.
        let starter = unknown.iter().any(|name| name == "todos.jsonl")
            && std::fs::read_to_string(std::path::Path::new(fixture).join("todos.jsonl")).is_ok_and(|text| text.contains("\"id\":\"todo-milk\""));
        let starter = if starter { format!("\n  hint: todos.jsonl is the todo starter's sample that `snapback4 init` wrote; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's {} while they are unmodified (or delete them, and the starter's list/add/toggle/remove lines, yourself)", super::test_cases::FILE) } else { String::new() };
        return Err(format!("{fixture}: {} name{} no table in snapback/*.q; nothing was run{starter}\n  hint: a fixture file is <table>.jsonl for a declared table ({}), plus {}; see snapback4 guide testing",
            unknown.join(", "), if unknown.len() == 1 { "s" } else { "" }, tables.join(", "), super::test_cases::FILE));
    }
    // Fixture rows take `/seed`'s insert path (defaults, sequences, unique with the author's codes, caps, maintained views), not the trusted bulk import.
    let loaded = engine.seed_at(rows, now).map_err(|error| {
        let text = error.to_string();
        let hint = if text.contains("NOT_FOUND") { "\n  hint: every fixture file loads as one batch, parents first; NOT_FOUND means a referenced or read-through parent row is in no fixture file (check the id)" } else { "" };
        format!("{fixture}: {text}{hint}")
    })?;
    if residency {
        engine.adopt_at(bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot build fixture maintains: {error}"))?;
    }
    if !options.json { println!("test: loaded {loaded} rows from {fixture} in {:.1} s", started.elapsed().as_secs_f64()); }
    Ok(engine)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#5-fixtures-and-dev-evidence — relative time leaves, seeds and operations share one captured clock.
/// Only typed time leaves interpret `now`: text and JSON retain their literals.
pub(crate) fn relative_times(table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table, row: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(fields) = row.as_object_mut() {
        for (name, value) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = table.columns.get(name) { time_value(kind, value, now).map_err(|why| format!("{name}: {why}"))?; }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub(super) fn time_value(kind: &snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType, value: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), value) if !value.is_null() => time_value(inner, value, now),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, serde_json::Value::Array(items)) => {
            for item in items { time_value(of, item, now)?; }
            Ok(())
        }
        (ColumnType::Time, value) => {
            let Some(text) = value.as_str().filter(|text| text.starts_with("now")) else { return Ok(()) };
            let delta = if text == "now" { 0 } else {
                let offset = text.strip_prefix("now").unwrap();
                if !offset.starts_with(['+', '-']) { return Err(format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?}: expected now or now±duration (ms, s, m, h, d, w)")); }
                crate::dev_clock::offset(offset).map_err(|why| format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?}: {}", why.message))?
            };
            let resolved = now.checked_add(delta).filter(|time| (0..=snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX).contains(time))
                .ok_or_else(|| format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?} is outside Unix milliseconds 0..9007199254740991"))?;
            *value = resolved.into();
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}
pub(super) fn argument_times(kinds: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(fields) = args.as_object_mut() {
        for (name, value) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = kinds.get(name).and_then(snapback4_core::schema::arg_as_column) {
                time_value(&kind, value, now).map_err(|why| format!("{name}: {why}"))?;
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod column_tests {
    use super::*;
    use snapback4_core::{Context, Value};
//! Narrow stable leading-require reduction for operation-specific authority.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#81-exact-conservative-boundary-review-clarification — unknown write sets never establish stability.
use super::*;
use crate::ast::{Stmt,ProgramDecl,ProgramKind,InvariantDecl,RuleDecl};
use super::super::catalog::TableInfo;
pub(super) fn resolve<'a>(catalog:&'a Catalog,decl:&InvariantDecl)->Option<(String,&'a ProgramDecl)>{
    if matches!(decl.target.as_str(),"read"|"insert"|"update"|"delete"|"always"|"write"){return None;}
    let matches=catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|{let module=std::path::Path::new(&d.file).file_stem().unwrap().to_string_lossy();d.programs.iter().map(move|p|(super::super::operation_name(&module,&p.name),p))})
        .filter(|(name,p)|p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&(name==&decl.target||p.name==decl.target)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if matches.len()==1 {matches.into_iter().next()}else{None}
}
pub(super) fn safe(rule:&RuleExpr)->bool {match rule{
    RuleExpr::Exists{key,equalities,..}=>key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).all(|t|matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_))),
    RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().all(safe),
    RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)=>[a,b].iter().all(|t|matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_))),
    RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Deny|RuleExpr::Public{..}=>true,_=>false,
}}
pub(super) fn requirements(catalog:&Catalog,info:&TableInfo,p:&ProgramDecl)->Vec<RuleExpr>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    for s in p.prelude.iter().chain(&p.body){let Stmt::Require{condition,site,..}=s else{break};
        let decl=RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),value:condition.clone(),site:site.clone()};
        if let Ok(rule)=super::super::schema::bind_rule(catalog,info,&decl)&&safe(&rule){out.push(rule);}
    }out
}
pub(super) fn prove(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant)->Result<bool,String>{
    let p=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|Some(&p.program.name)==inv.operation.as_ref()).ok_or("operation did not bind")?;
    if !safe(inv.policy.as_ref().ok_or("unsupported policy")?){return Err("operation policy needs unsupported target-image, negative-probe or live-reference bindings".into());}
    let writes=snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program);
    if !writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table){return Err("operation has no direct write to the named table; implicit effects require a reduction".into());}
    let dependencies=inv.requirements.iter().flat_map(kernel::dependencies).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    if writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|dependencies.contains(t)) {return Err("require dependency can be changed by this operation, including its target write".into());}
    if bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|!t.maintained.is_empty()||!t.sequences.is_empty()||t.on_delete.values().any(|a|a.writes())) || !bound.schema.maintains.is_empty(){return Err("implicit maintain/sequence/referential writes require dependency stability reduction".into());}
    // Source helpers/control flow may hide write sets or viewer changes.
    let json=serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap();
    fn opaque(v:&serde_json::Value)->bool{match v{serde_json::Value::Object(m)=>m.keys().any(|k|matches!(k.as_str(),"For"|"If"|"JobAs"|"JobQuery"|"Emit"))||m.values().any(opaque),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(opaque),_=>false}}
    if opaque(&json){return Err("operation control flow or opaque effects are outside stable leading-require reduction".into());}
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(inv.requirements.clone());
    // Table-rule gates can only further restrict a dominating stable require;
    // using the require alone overapproximates effective owner-field gates.
    kernel::implies(&admission,inv.policy.as_ref().unwrap())
}

The main change is the acceptance boundary: section 13 makes an authored operation from a generated fixture fail acceptance, while keeping seed-only and synthetic probes non-failing. The separate origin label preserves that distinction. I’m checking whether the reporting contract makes it clear enough, and whether the bounded `check` path can omit a failure from normal output.

//! Author-owned intent starts with a reviewable draft, never inferred policy.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#103-adoption — syntactic coverage is distinct from a proved invariant.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn missing(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name,t)| {
        ([&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|!matches!(r,RuleExpr::Deny)) || !t.written_by.is_empty())
            && !bound.invariants.iter().any(|i| &i.table==*name)
    }).map(|(name,_)|name.clone()).collect()
}

/// Questions and independent role/column candidates, never copies of a gate.
/// The caller must keep this outside the compiled .q source set.
pub fn draft(bound:&Bound)->String{
    let mut out=String::from("-- DRAFT: NOT ACCEPTED. Confirm intent against the APP SPECIFICATION.\n-- These role/column alternatives are questions, not inferred policy.\n-- Select intended audiences, resource relationships and lifecycle conditions.\n-- Copy confirmed declarations into authored snapback/*.q; this draft is never compiled.\n-- snapback4 guide security\n\n");
    let mut roles=Vec::new();
    for (roster,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
        for column in super::privilege::role_columns(bound,roster){
            let Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum{variants})=t.columns.get(&column).map(|k|k.required())else{continue;};
            let Some(principal)=super::privilege::global_principal(bound,roster)else{continue;};
            for role in variants {roles.push(format!("exists {roster}[{principal} = viewer, {column} = '{}']",role.replace('\'',"\\'")));}
        }
    }
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
        out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}: who may read each field, create, edit, delete? Which current relationship must still hold?\n"));
        for (column,kind) in &t.columns {
            if column=="id"||t.engine_field(column){continue;}
            out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{column} ({kind}): which roles may see this value? May outsiders see its existence?\n"));
            for candidate in &roles {
                // Keep candidates commented: mutually exclusive roles cannot all be requirements.
                if ![&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|r.quarry()==*candidate){out.push_str(&format!("-- Confirm/combine audience alternative: invariant {name}.{column} read only {candidate}\n"));}
            }
        }
        for effect in ["insert","update","delete"] {
            if covered(bound,name,effect){continue;}
            out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{effect}: name the permitted appointer/owner and lifecycle condition from the spec.\n"));
        }
        for (column,authority) in &t.written_by {out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{column}: is .{} still the intended editor? Use column update intent for edits only; write also includes creation.\n",authority.owner));}
    }
    out
}

fn covered(bound:&Bound,table:&str,effect:&str)->bool{
    bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==table && i.operation.is_none() && i.column.is_none()
        && (i.target==effect || i.target=="write" && matches!(effect,"insert"|"update")))
}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct UncoveredExposure {pub table:String,pub effect:String,pub audience:String,pub row_predicate:String,pub column:Option<String>}
pub(super) fn uncovered(bound:&Bound,exposures:&[Exposure])->Vec<UncoveredExposure>{
    let mut out=exposures.iter().flat_map(|x|x.effects.iter().filter(|(effect,rule)|rule.as_str()!="deny"&&!covered(bound,&x.table,effect)).map(|(effect,predicate)|UncoveredExposure{
        table:x.table.clone(),effect:effect.clone(),audience:if predicate=="allow"||predicate.starts_with("public"){"every signed-in user".into()}else{format!("caller satisfying {predicate}")},row_predicate:predicate.clone(),column:None,
    })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (column,authority) in &t.written_by{
        if covered(bound,name,"update")||bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==*name&&i.operation.is_none()&&i.column.as_ref()==Some(column)&&i.target=="write"){continue;}
        out.push(UncoveredExposure{table:name.clone(),effect:"update".into(),column:Some(column.clone()),audience:format!("owner .{} = viewer",authority.owner),row_predicate:format!("field-owner patch authority for {column}; table update rule may deny")});
    }}out
}
